## 2 Coordination (2026-09-19: P4 agreed; supervisor rulings applied; F-CTR agreed; ENG-B1 agreed)

**lane-p4-doctor (SOP-1 registry shape).** Proposal: P4 owns the T-PLT-39 revision (adding the FK behind the placeholder
`close_checklist_item.control_execution_id`), `tables/platform.py::control_execution`, `controls/evidence.py::record_execution`,
routes API-R-52, and the producers whose code exists (CTL-001/002/012/022/029/030/039/044). F-CLO owns the producers of
CTL-016 (lock execution CLO-6, close run CLO-19), CTL-019/020 (CLO-10, `JOURNAL_RUN`), CTL-021 (CLO-14, `JOURNAL_BATCH`),
CTL-024/025/026 (CLO-16/17). F-CLO adds no migration in this phase and expects none in CLO-5 to CLO-25 (close tables landed
in `0047`), so P4 takes the migration head. Signature F-CLO will call:

```
record_execution(uow, *, control_id: str, run_ref_type: RunRefType, run_ref_id: UUID,
                 population_count: int, exception_count: int, result: ControlResult,
                 detail: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, exceptions_file_id: UUID | None = None,
                 entity_id: UUID | None = None, book_code: str | None = None,
                 period_id: UUID | None = None) -> UUID
```

Helper-side validation proposed: `control_id` matches `^CTL-[0-9]{3}$` and exists in `controls.yaml` (ValueError); `PASS`
requires `exception_count == 0`; `FAIL` requires `exception_count >= 1`; `NOT_APPLICABLE` requires `population_count == 0`;
`engine_release_id` and `executed_at` filled by the helper; `RunRefType` is a `StrEnum` in `erev_api.controls.evidence`, not
`erev_api.enums` (P4 adjustment: T-PLT-39 `run_ref_type` is a text column with a CHECK list, not a 04 §3 E-enum, so an enum
in `erev_api.enums` would fail DG-ARC-09 `test_dg_arc_09_enums_match_data_model`); its values are the eight T-PLT-39 literals
plus `RECONCILIATION_RUN` and `PERIOD_LOCK` (04 rev 1.18, P4's docs-first commit citing rulings (a) and (b)); F-CLO producers
import `from erev_api.controls.evidence import RunRefType, record_execution`; no unique index on (control, run_ref_type,
run_ref_id), matching
04 `ix_control_execution__run` (a re-run appends a row; IM-A). `detail` shapes are per control and owned by the producer's
lane (§11). Two questions raised with P4 were returned to the supervisor and ruled (§11 Q-1, Q-2).

**P4's reply (lane-p4-doctor).** Agreed as the interface on every point: ownership split, the keyword-only signature
returning the row id, helper-side validation, no unique run index, shape-agnostic `detail`, tagging. Caveat: the T-PLT-39
registry is not in P4's current dispatch (P4b covers SAR-40 collectors, CLI composition and an execution/audit inventory);
P4 records the shape in `docs/reviews/loop/prod/P4-doctor.md` (commit `90331d1`) and builds to it when SOP-1 is dispatched. F-CLO tells P4
before any CLO item adds a migration (none expected). `test_ctl_042_every_producer_records_execution` will cover only
producers whose code exists on main when written, so CLO-6 is not its prerequisite.

**Supervisor rulings on the two questions (relayed by the team lead and by P4).** (a) A reconciliation generated on demand
(CTL-024/025/026) records its control execution against the reconciliation run's own row id with `run_ref_type =
RECONCILIATION_RUN`: an on-demand run still creates a run row, and no execution row exists without a run reference.
(b) CTL-016 at lock time before any close run exists (pre-CLO-19) binds to the `PERIOD_LOCK` approval request id with
`run_ref_type = PERIOD_LOCK`: the control attaches to the lock decision; once CLO-19 exists, lock-time executions inside
a close run bind to the close run. Both literals join T-PLT-39 `run_ref_type` when P4 implements the registry (04
amendment by the supervisor). Applied in §9 and §10.

**lane-p3-terraform (F-CTR preparation, worktree `l20`) on the shared `approvals/subjects.py`.** Agreed: each lane
removes only its own `PENDING_SUBJECTS` entries (F-CTR `CONTRACT_VOID`; F-CLO `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` in CLO-12,
`PERIOD_LOCK` in CLO-6, `PERIOD_REOPEN` in CLO-7, tuple kept sorted); F-CTR's `CONTRACT_VOID` `SubjectSpec` goes directly
after `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION`, F-CLO's three entries append after `JOURNAL_RUN` at the end of the dict; neither lane edits
`SubjectSpec`, `register_lifecycle` or `_registered*` without telling the other first; entity attribution for readiness
row APR-SUBJECTS-CLO is `period_state.entity_id` for the two period subjects and `manual_adjustment.entity_id` for the
adjustment, with `amount_functional` and `revenue_affecting = True` on `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` only. This phase touches
`subjects.py` nowhere.

**lane-eng-b1's reply (F-RPS / ENG-E1, `sprint/l17`; recorded as §8 of `docs/reviews/loop/prod/F-RPS-ENG-E1-prep.md`).**
(1) F-RPS owns the registry `backend/erev_api/domain/reports/snapshots.py` (to be written) exposing
`SNAPSHOT_DATASETS: Mapping[str, DatasetBuilder]` keyed by the E-64 literal, with `DatasetBuilder =
Callable[[UnitOfWork, SnapshotScope], Encoded]`, `SnapshotScope(entity_id, book_code, period_id, known_at)` and the
engine dataclass `erev_engine.stages.s15_disclosures.snapshots.Encoded(kind, content: bytes (S15-R-18 UTF-8 CSV),
file_sha256, row_count, control_totals)` from the pure `encode(Dataset, control_totals)`; the kind set is the engine
constant `SNAPSHOT_KINDS` (12, E-64 order) and the manifest is `manifest_sha256({kind: file_sha256})` (S15-R-19). F-RPS
owns all twelve builders because each kind's bytes must equal the report the disclosure pack and the "as locked" runs
show (S15-INV-06): `WATERFALL`, `CONTRACT_BALANCES`, `CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD`, `RPO`, `RPO_ROLLFORWARD`,
`DISAGGREGATION` (registered today); `PRIOR_PERIOD_POB_REVENUE` (EDS-4 / RPS-3, ENG-C4 first); `COST_ROLLFORWARD` (RPT-32,
on `l17` at `82f98a8`, registration pending a ruling); `JE_POPULATION`, `OUT_OF_PERIOD_REGISTER`, `MODIFICATION_REGISTER`,
`MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_REGISTER` (RPS-8 register builders, BUILD_SPEC :8961, although their rows come from close-domain
tables). F-RPS also owns the read side (`read_snapshot(session, period_lock_id, kind) -> Encoded` for report runs with
`period_lock_id`, `framework.py:514`). F-CLO owns none of the twelve. (2) The lock snapshot is the S15-R-18 CSV built
directly inside the lock transaction with `report_run_id` NULL (T-CLS-05 nullable; BR-PLT-04 forbids awaiting a job-path
run; S15-INV-06 wants one artefact and one hash per kind, distinct from the per-run JSON dataset of `framework.py:797`);
no synchronous create-and-render entry point is needed. A run row per kind for audit navigation would be a supervisor
ruling and would leave the registry contract unchanged; ruled NULL (Q-10). (3) None of the five platform keys drives a period to
`closed` or `reopened` (all `period_states: []`, event-only timelines); the runner's literals are the E-04 lowercase
values, matching SM-07; the PRP keys are not consumers of CLO-6/7.

**F-CLO contract for CLO-6 `snapshots.freeze_datasets` and CLO-20 `DATASET_FREEZE` (from (1) and (2)).** Iterate
`SNAPSHOT_KINDS`; for each kind call `SNAPSHOT_DATASETS[kind](uow, scope)`; write `content` as a `file_object` of purpose
`SNAPSHOT_DATASET`; insert the T-CLS-05 row with `snapshot_kind`, `file_id`, `file_sha256`, `row_count`, `control_totals`
and `report_run_id` NULL; store `period_lock.snapshot_manifest_sha256 = manifest_sha256({kind: file_sha256})`; a kind
missing from the registry is a hard lock failure with no partial manifest (GATE-EDS made concrete). `close_run.counts.datasets`
(T-CLS-01) maps each kind to the same hash as of `cutoff_known_at`, and the lock reuses the frozen files when no group
became dirty after it (BS4-D-04, CLO-20 `test_lock_marks_lock_step`).

**lane-eng-b1 (F-RPS / ENG-E1 interfaces).** Asked: which side owns each of the twelve E-64 dataset kinds (proposal: F-RPS
owns a `SNAPSHOT_DATASETS: Mapping[SnapshotKind, DatasetBuilder]` registry under `erev_api/domain/reports`, with
`DatasetBuilder(uow, *, entity_id, book_code, period_id, known_at) -> Dataset(kind, csv_bytes, row_count, control_totals,
file_sha256, report_run_id | None)` over the EDS-6 encoder; F-CLO's `close/snapshots.py` iterates the kinds, writes the
`SNAPSHOT_DATASET` files, inserts `lock_snapshot` and computes the manifest); whether `lock_snapshot.report_run_id` is null
(S15-R-18 CSV built directly) or a synchronous report run per kind (`framework.py` today stamps a JSON dataset per run,
`dataset_bytes` :797); whether any of the five platform keys drives a period to `closed` or `reopened` (then CLO-6/7 are
their dependency and the `period_states` block literals must match SM-07). Heads-up given on the D-91 C606-04 RPO rollforward
basis for `builders/rpo_rollforward.py`.

## 4 CLO-5 data-quality monitors (table 15.4-E; REQ-CLS-019)

Inputs are frozen records the DB producer (`run_monitors`, CLO-5) assembles; the pure module never reads a session. Every
finding carries `source = DATA_QUALITY`, `code`, exception severity by E-43 (`ERROR → BLOCKING`, `WARNING → WARNING`),
`dedupe_key = "DATA_QUALITY:<code>:<subject>"` (T-IMP-05), `title` from the catalogue at creation (SCREENS OQ-S-21), and
`message` naming the subject. Running twice yields the same keys (`test_monitor_rerun_is_idempotent`). Thresholds read
T-PLT-31 `close.dq_revenue_without_billing_days` (default 60) and `close.dq_inactive_contract_days` (default 90); "more than N
days" means strictly greater (61 raises, 60 does not; 91 raises, 90 does not).

| Code | Default severity | Input record | Predicate | Subject of the dedupe key |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DQ_DUPLICATE_INVOICE` | ERROR | `SourceInvoiceRef(source_system, external_invoice_id, external_version, row_id, customer_ref?, invoice_number?, amount?, issue_date?)` | ruled Q-8: (1) more than one distinct `row_id` for one (`source_system`, `external_invoice_id`, `external_version`); or (2) distinct `external_invoice_id` values with identical (`customer_ref`, `invoice_number`, `amount`, `issue_date`), all four present. A later `external_version` of the same (`source_system`, `external_invoice_id`) is a version or correction path, not a duplicate. The spec owner confirms the wording at the CLO-5 build | (1) `<source_system>:<external_invoice_id>:<external_version>`; (2) `<customer_ref>:<invoice_number>:<amount>:<issue_date ISO>` |
| `DQ_REVENUE_WITHOUT_BILLING` | WARNING | `UnbilledRevenueRef(contract_id, obligation_id, first_recognition_date, first_billing_date)` | `first_billing_date is None` and `(period_end - first_recognition_date).days > N` | `<obligation_id>` |
| `DQ_NEGATIVE_LIABILITY_LAYER` | ERROR | `LiabilityLayerRef(contract_id, layer_key, open_balance, currency)` (T-CON-18 rows with `balance_role = CONTRACT_LIABILITY`, projected to the period end) | `open_balance < 0` | `<layer_key>` |
| `DQ_RECOGNITION_AFTER_POB_END` | WARNING | `RecognitionRef(contract_id, obligation_id, obligation_end_date, period_start, amount)` | `amount != 0` and `period_start > obligation_end_date` | `<obligation_id>:<period_start ISO>` |
| `DQ_INACTIVE_CONTRACT` | WARNING | `ContractActivityRef(contract_id, is_active, last_event_date)` | `is_active` and (`last_event_date is None` or `(period_end - last_event_date).days > N`) | `<contract_id>` |
| `FX_RATE_MISSING` | ERROR | `FxRequirementRef(contract_id, txn_currency, functional_currency, rate_date, rate_present)` | `txn_currency != functional_currency` and not `rate_present` | `<txn_currency>:<functional_currency>:<rate_date ISO>` |

Severity override: a published tenant `DATA_QUALITY` rule (T-REF-24 to -26) binds to a monitor by `rule_key = <code>` and
sets `outputs.severity` to `ERROR` or `WARNING` (`rule_sets.DATA_QUALITY_SEVERITIES`); `erev_engine.rules.FIELDS[DATA_QUALITY]`
is empty, so binding by key is the mechanism. Ruled Q-4: provisioning seeds a system `DATA_QUALITY` rule set, code
`DQ-SYSTEM`, `PUBLISHED`, with the six monitor rules keyed by code (owner F-CLO with the provisioning module, CLO-5; docs
first in 04 and 05). Gate
signal: `DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR` is `PASSED` (count 0, detail None) when no `BLOCKING` `DATA_QUALITY` exception item of the period
is `OPEN` or `IN_PROGRESS`, else `FAILED` with count n and detail `"Data-quality errors: <n>"` (SCREENS_B :368). `WARNING`
findings never block lock. Notification (ruled Q-5): NTF-11 `EXCEPTION_ASSIGNED` to the item's owner when set, else to the
tenant's Revenue Accountants; CLO-5's acceptance and NTF-11 compose that way. **Rule (ruled Q-11, D-98 candidate 55):**
an open `DATA_QUALITY` item whose condition no longer holds on a re-run stays open until a user resolves, waives or
dismisses it (an auditable human step; T-IMP-05 `resolution`, SM exception rows). The monitor never resolves an item
automatically: automatic resolution would be a new system transition and is not specified.

## 5 SM-07 close-gate state machine (pure `period_machine.decide`)

`TRANSITIONS` (from → to by command), equal to `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` of `domain/reference/periods.py` and the DB-07 pairs:
OPEN `future → open`; START_CLOSE `open|reopened → closing`; CANCEL_CLOSE `closing → open`; LOCK `closing → closed`;
REOPEN `closed → reopened`; PERMANENT_LOCK `closed → permanently_locked`. Every other (state, command) pair is
`Refusal("invalid-transition", rule_id "DB-07", code "EREV-PER-001")` with the command's message (`close/commands.py`
`NOT_STARTABLE`, `NOT_CLOSING`; new `NOT_LOCKABLE` "Only a period in soft close locks. {period_key} is {state}.",
`NOT_REOPENABLE` "Only a closed period reopens. {period_key} is {state}.", `NOT_PERMANENTLY_LOCKABLE` "Only a closed period is
permanently locked. {period_key} is {state}."; `periods.NOT_FUTURE` for OPEN). Each accepted transition writes one
`period_state_transition` row and one audit event with user, reason code and UTC time (REQ-CLS-001; `_move` today).

| Command (route) | Guards in order | Refusal (status slug; detail; rule id) | Effects |
|---|---|---|---|
| REQUEST_LOCK (`POST /periods/{id}/request-lock`) | state `closing`; `certification_comment` ≥ 10 characters (BR-PLT-08); every gate other than `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED` `PASSED` (BR-CLS-01) | 409 `invalid-transition`; 422 `validation-failed` field `certification_comment` rule `BR-PLT-08`; 409 `close-gates-failed` detail `"<n> close gates have not passed: <label list>."` with one `errors[]` entry per failing gate (`rule_id` = gate check code, `message` = gate detail), labels joined by `", "` in T-CLS-02 order (ERR-14; BS4-D-08; SCREENS_B :400) | `PERIOD_LOCK` request, one step `period.lock` narrowed to role `controller`, `min_approvers 1` (PRD :285); response `{approval_request_id, gate_results}` |
| LOCK execution (approval handler) | approver ≠ requester (BR-CLS-02; `self-approval`); approver holds `period.lock` for the entity; TOTP within 5 minutes (BR-PLT-06; ERR-28 `mfa-step-up-required`); gates re-evaluated on the canonical thirteen of `provisioning.SYSTEM_CLOSE_GATES` (F-CLO-R1), every gate other than `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED` `PASSED` | 403 `self-approval`; 403 `forbidden`; 403 `mfa-step-up-required`; 409 `close-gates-failed` with the request left `PENDING`, no void (ruled Q-3) | `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED` → `PASSED`; `period_lock` kind `LOCK` with `certification` (13 results), `ledger_head_chain_seq`, `audit_head_*`, `snapshot_manifest_sha256`; 12 `lock_snapshot` rows; reconciliations `REVIEWED`/`AUTO_CERTIFIED` → `CERTIFIED` with `period_lock_id`, `certified_at`; state `closed`; next period `future → open` (BR-CLS-03); NTF-06 |
| REQUEST_REOPEN (`/request-reopen`) | state `closed`; `reason_code` ∈ {`ERROR_CORRECTION`, `LATE_SOURCE_DATA`, `AUDIT_ADJUSTMENT`, `OTHER`}; `comment` ≥ 10; no later period of the entity and book `closed` or `permanently_locked` (BR-CLS-05) | 409 `invalid-transition`; 422 `validation-failed` rule `REASON_CODE_NOT_ALLOWED`; 422 `validation-failed` field `comment` rule `BR-PLT-08`; 409 `later-period-closed` detail `"Reopen <later period> first. A later period of <entity> in book <book> is closed."` (ERR-16) | `PERIOD_REOPEN` request, one step `period.reopen_approve`, `min_approvers 2`, at least one approver `controller`, neither the requester (PRD :286; D-75 Q12) |
| REOPEN execution | requester not among the approvers; BR-CLS-05 re-checked on the fact supplied at execution (F-CLO-R2); quorum of §6 met | 403 `self-approval`; 409 `later-period-closed`; stays `PENDING` otherwise | `period_lock` kind `REOPEN` with `previous_lock_id`, `reason_code`; snapshots kept; state `reopened`; NTF-07 |
| REQUEST_PERMANENT_LOCK (`/request-permanent-lock`) | state `closed`; `comment` ≥ 10; every earlier period of the entity and book `permanently_locked` | 409 `invalid-transition` (pair); 422 `validation-failed` rule `BR-PLT-08`; earlier-period guard refusal: 409 `invalid-transition` with message `"Permanently lock <earlier period> first. Every earlier period of <entity> in book <book> must be permanently locked."` (ruled Q-6; rule id SM-07) | `PERIOD_LOCK` request with lock kind `PERMANENT_LOCK`, one step `period.lock` role `controller` |
| PERMANENT_LOCK execution | approver ≠ requester ("second Controller"); step-up | 403 `self-approval`; 403 `mfa-step-up-required` | `period_lock` kind `PERMANENT_LOCK`; state `permanently_locked`; later `request-reopen` → 409 `invalid-transition` |
| START_CLOSE, CANCEL_CLOSE, OPEN | as built by CLO-3 (`commands.start_close`, `cancel_close`, `reference.periods.open`) | unchanged | unchanged; BR-DAT-07 while `closing` |

## 6 Approval state machines (pure `approvals/quorum`)

Decision refusal order (engine `decide`, DG-KRN-APR-02, reproduced by `quorum.refusal`): non-human principal → 403
`forbidden`; request not `PENDING` → 409 `invalid-transition`; step permission not held for the entity → 403 `forbidden` (404
outside scope); step role not held → 403 `forbidden`; MFA → today 403 `mfa-required` when `mfa_verified_at is None`, BR-PLT-06
requires 403 `mfa-step-up-required` when the verification is older than `mfa.STEP_UP_WINDOW` (5 minutes) for approving any
request, locking and reopening (Q-7); approver or delegator is the preparer or an excluded decider → 403 `self-approval`;
earlier decision on the request → 409 `approver-already-decided`; rejection without comment → 422 `validation-failed`;
then the content-hash check → 409 `stale-approval` with the request voided `STALE_SUBJECT` (BR-PLT-04).

**Step-up specification (ruled spec-only; supervisor D-98 candidate "APR-STEPUP"; not implemented in this lane).**
`engine.decide` keeps its presence check (`mfa_verified_at is None` → 403 `mfa-required`, ERR-27 "Verify with your
authenticator app…") and adds, immediately after it, the freshness check `not mfa.step_up_fresh_at(mfa_verified_at,
uow.now)` (window `mfa.STEP_UP_WINDOW` = 5 minutes) → 403 `mfa-step-up-required`, ERR-28 "Enter a code from your
authenticator app to continue." Affected: every decision on every E-08 subject (30 values of `ApprovalSubjectType`,
BR-PLT-06 "approving any request"), and the executions of `PERIOD_LOCK` (lock, permanent lock) and `PERIOD_REOPEN`
through their decisions; `auth/api_clients.py` already applies the same window for API-client creation. Tests that pin
today's presence-only behaviour and move with the ruling: `tests/domain/platform/test_approval_engine.py`,
`tests/api/test_cockpit.py`, `tests/api/test_mfa.py`, `tests/api/test_operator_tenants.py`, `tests/unit/test_openapi_export.py`
(each names `mfa-required`). Owning test once ruled: CLO-6 `test_lock_decided_by_another_controller` under CTL-016.
`quorum.refusal` encodes the ruled order with `step_up_required = True`; `False` reproduces the engine as built (the
ruled interim). Owner of the engine change: F-ADM (approvals owner), D-98 candidate APR-STEPUP.

Step completion (`quorum.step_outcome`): REJECT → `REJECTED`; APPROVE count ≥ `min_approvers` and (`role_required_any` is
None or some approver holds it) → `APPROVED`; otherwise `PENDING`. `routing.StepPlan.role_id` narrows every approver of a
step (CTR-9), so "at least one Controller of two" needs the any-approver predicate in `engine._advance`; it is a semantics
change owned by CLO-7 under CTL-018 (`test_ctl_018_reopen_needs_a_controller`: two Revenue Reviewer approvals leave the
request `PENDING` and the period `closed`; a Controller's approval completes it). Ruled 2026-09-19: the predicate is
specified here and in `quorum.REOPEN_QUORUM` / `period_machine._reopen` for CLO-7 to implement under CTL-018; the
supervisor carries the engine change as a second D-98 candidate.

| Subject | Routing (PRD §2.5) | Quorum | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PERIOD_LOCK` (`LOCK`, `PERMANENT_LOCK`) | 1 step `period.lock`, role `controller` | `Quorum(1, None)` on a role-narrowed step | preparer = requester; BR-CLS-02 |
| `PERIOD_REOPEN` | 1 step `period.reopen_approve`, `min_approvers 2` | `Quorum(2, "controller")` | neither approver is the requester (engine preparer check); `period.reopen_approve` held by `controller` and `revenue_reviewer` (PRD §5.6 :1800–1801) |
| `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` | `amount_functional_abs < 10,000.00` USD: 1 step `adjustment.approve`; `≥ 10,000.00`: attachment required, 2 steps `adjustment.approve`, second role `controller` | `Quorum(1, None)` per step | 422 `validation-failed` field `attachments` on submit above threshold without one (CLO-12); `revenue_affecting` preview required (REQ-PLT-015); BR-CLS-06 in a `reopened` period: no auto-approval, approver ≠ submitter |
| `JOURNAL_RUN` | 1 step `journal.approve` | `Quorum(1, None)` | approver ≠ runner (BR-JE-01); built (CLO-11) |
| `EXCEPTION_WAIVER` | per DIN-11 | `Quorum(1, None)` with `excluded_deciders` (item owner) | built |

## 11 Questions returned and the supervisor's rulings (2026-09-19; Q-1 to Q-10 all ruled; D-98 candidates; none decided by this lane)

- **Q-1** Ruled 2026-09-19: on-demand reconciliations (CTL-024/025/026) record against the reconciliation run's own row id with `run_ref_type = RECONCILIATION_RUN` (T-PLT-39 gains the literal through P4's SOP-1 work). Applied in §9, §10.
- **Q-2** Ruled 2026-09-19: before CLO-19, CTL-016 at lock time binds to the `PERIOD_LOCK` approval request id with `run_ref_type = PERIOD_LOCK`; inside a close run it binds to the close run. Applied in §9, §10.
- **Q-3** Ruled: a lock execution that finds a gate failing after the request refuses with 409 `close-gates-failed` and the request stays `PENDING` (no void). `period_machine._lock` behaves so. Applied in §5.
- **Q-4** Ruled: provisioning seeds a system `DATA_QUALITY` rule set, code `DQ-SYSTEM`, `PUBLISHED`, so the CLO-5 monitors bind by `rule_key`; owner F-CLO with the provisioning module; docs first in 04 and 05 at the CLO-5 build. Applied in §4.
- **Q-5** Ruled: `DATA_QUALITY` items notify the item's owner when set, else the tenant's Revenue Accountants (CLO-5 and NTF-11 compose that way). Applied in §4.
- **Q-6** Ruled: `request-permanent-lock` with an earlier period not `permanently_locked` refuses 409 `invalid-transition` with the text "Permanently lock <earlier> first. Every earlier period of <entity> in book <book> must be permanently locked." (`period_machine.PERMANENT_LOCK_ORDER`). Applied in §5.
- **Q-7** Ruled: specification only in this lane (§6 step-up specification, exact 403 codes and affected subjects); no implementation here; D-98 candidate "APR-STEPUP" owned by F-ADM; `quorum.refusal`'s flag reproducing today's behaviour is the interim.
- **Q-8** Ruled: a later `external_version` of the same (`source_system`, `external_invoice_id`) is a version or correction path, not `DQ_DUPLICATE_INVOICE`; the duplicate rule fires on the same (`source_system`, `external_invoice_id`, `external_version`) appearing twice, or on distinct external ids with identical (customer, invoice number, amount, date); the spec owner confirms the wording at the CLO-5 build. Applied in §4 and in `monitor_rules` (commit below).
- **Q-9** Ruled: this file is the preparation record; `docs/reviews/loop/sprint/F-CLO.md` (the brief's path) is the build-lane record.
- **Q-10** Ruled 2026-09-19 (supervisor; D-98 candidate): `lock_snapshot.report_run_id` stays NULL for lock snapshots. A report run row per kind would be a synthetic run with no user command behind it and would blur the RPT audit trail; audit navigation for "as locked" data goes through the T-CLS-05 rows and the S15-R-19 manifest (the F-RPS read side), not through report runs. The §2 contract stands unchanged.

## 16 CLO-5 implementation slice (dispatch after the merge at main `4141f322`; l18 fast-forwarded to it)

Scope: BUILD_SPEC CLO-5 (`docs/BUILD_SPEC.md:8088`, fragment `docs/build-spec/14-close-reports-ai-demo-release.md`) —
`backend/erev_api/domain/close/monitors.py::run_monitors(uow, entity_id, book_code, period_id) -> MonitorRun` as the
real close-monitor entry point; the six table 15.4-E monitors evaluated through the reviewed `monitor_rules`; exception
persistence with deduplication through `imports.exceptions.raise_exception_item` (one open item per rule and subject;
a re-run counts the open item again and creates nothing); severity overrides from published `DATA_QUALITY` rule
versions; notifications per ruling Q-5; the real `DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR` signal in `gates.signals` / `gates.gate_results`
replacing the `FAILED` / "not evaluated" placeholder; the `DQ-SYSTEM` seed in provisioning (ruling Q-4; docs first in 04
§14.3 and dev-guide DG-KRN-TEN-01). Prerequisite CLO-4 (ticked) respected; no CLO-6/7 dependency is stubbed: GATE-EDS
(EDS-4/5/6) stays the acceptance prerequisite of CLO-6/7 and nothing here claims it.

### 16.1 Producer design (`close/monitors.py`)

| Step | Source and rule |
|---|---|
| Scope | `gates.scope_of_period(session, entity_id, book_code, period_id)`; 404 `not-found` when absent; monitors run only for `open`, `closing` and `reopened` periods (`gates.EVALUATED_STATES`); the period bounds are `scope.start_date` / `scope.end_date` |
| Settings | `registry.resolve` of T-PLT-31 `close.dq_revenue_without_billing_days` and `close.dq_inactive_contract_days` for the entity and book at `known_at = uow.now`; `monitor_rules.SETTING_RANGES` (F-CLO-R3) applies to the resolved value |
| Overrides | every `PUBLISHED` `DATA_QUALITY` `rule_set_version` in force at `uow.now` (`effective_from` / `effective_to` null or containing the date), highest `version_no` per set; rules whose `rule_key` is a monitor code contribute `outputs.severity`; sets are applied with `DQ-SYSTEM` first so a tenant's own set overrides the seed |
| `DQ_DUPLICATE_INVOICE` | `source_invoice` rows of the entity (`legal_entity_code = scope.entity_code`) as `SourceInvoiceRef(source_system, external_invoice_id, external_version, row id, customer_external_id or customer_id, invoice_number, total_amount, issue_date)`; the Q-8 predicates of `monitor_rules` |
| `DQ_REVENUE_WITHOUT_BILLING` | `subledger_line` of the entity and book with `effective_date <= period end`: per obligation the first `REVENUE_RECOGNITION` line date and the first `BILLING` line date (an obligation-level billing line, or a contract-level billing line with a null obligation, covers the obligation) |
| `DQ_NEGATIVE_LIABILITY_LAYER` | INTERIM source (corrected 2026-09-20, Codex R4): T-CON-18 `fx_layer_movement` is not built (BUILD_SPEC CTR-14 owns it), so the collector reads the current `contract_version_balance.contract_liability_txn` per contract of the entity and book, one aggregate layer `CONTRACT_LIABILITY:<contract external_id>`; a negative aggregate is flagged, and offsetting layers inside one contract stay undetected by this substitute until CTR-14 lands and the collector moves to the layer rows (Σ `amount_txn` per `layer_key`) |
| `DQ_RECOGNITION_AFTER_POB_END` | `schedule_line` rows of the period and entity/book with `amount != 0`, `subject_type = obligation`, of the latest version, joined to `obligation_version.end_date` (same version) |
| `DQ_INACTIVE_CONTRACT` | `ACTIVE` contracts of the entity with `max(contract_event.effective_date)` (null when no event) |
| `FX_RATE_MISSING` | distinct `transaction_currency` of the entity's `ACTIVE` contracts other than `legal_entity.functional_currency`; present when `fx.EFFECTIVE_RATES` has a `closing` rate `base → quote` at the period end with `resolution_rank = 1` (the same in-force rule as `fx.rate`) |
| Persistence | one `raise_exception_item` per finding: source `DATA_QUALITY`, the finding's code, E-43 severity, message, `dedupe = finding.dedupe_key` (T-IMP-05 `DATA_QUALITY:<code>:<subject>`), `business_key = subject`, `contract_id` / `obligation_id`, `entity_id`, `period_id`; the shared helper's partial unique index on open items makes a re-run count the item again (`occurrence_count + 1`, `last_seen_at`) and create nothing |
| Notification | for each item created: NTF-11 `EXCEPTION_ASSIGNED` to the item's owner when set, else to the tenant's Revenue Accountants (`role_holders(role_codes=["revenue_accountant"])`; ruling Q-5), title "Exception: <code>", body the message, link `/data/exceptions/<id>` |
| Gate | `gates.signals` counts open (`OPEN` / `IN_PROGRESS`) `BLOCKING` `DATA_QUALITY` items of the entity and period into `CloseSignals.data_quality_blocking`; `gate_results` emits `monitor_rules.data_quality_gate(count, at)` (`PASSED` 0 / `FAILED` n "Data-quality errors: n") |
| Wiring | `commands.refresh_checklist` (the SYSTEM-principal refresh of `GET /periods/{id}/cockpit`) runs the monitors before `evaluate_gates` for an evaluated state; the close run's `EXCEPTION_CHECK` step (CLO-19) calls the same function later |
| Audit | one event `close.run_monitors` on the `period_state` with the finding, blocking, warning, created, seen-again and open-blocking counts |

**Ruled Q-11 (D-98 candidate 55): no automatic resolution.** An open `DATA_QUALITY` item whose condition no longer holds
on a re-run stays open until a user resolves, waives or dismisses it (an auditable human step); the monitor counts it
again only while the condition holds and never closes it. `monitors.run_monitors` behaves so (it raises and re-counts, and
resolves nothing).

## 17 CLO-6 source slice (dispatch after the CLO-5 record; base `b1167410`)

Scope: BUILD_SPEC CLO-6 (`docs/BUILD_SPEC.md:8117`) — `request_lock`, `request_permanent_lock`, the `PERIOD_LOCK` approval
handler (lock execution) in `close/commands.py`; `close/snapshots.py` (`freeze_datasets`, `write_lock_snapshots`);
`close/queries.py::list_locks`; the `PERIOD_LOCK` `SubjectSpec` (removed from `PENDING_SUBJECTS`, appended after
`JOURNAL_RUN` per the F-CTR agreement); API-R-18 `POST /periods/{id}/request-lock`, `/request-permanent-lock`,
`GET /periods/{id}/locks`; the twelve CLO-6 acceptance tests written (DB-bound; NOT RUN here). The path consumes the carried
agreements of §16.3 exactly and, where a producer another lane owns is absent on this tree, **refuses by name** through
`close/dependencies.py::ProducerMissing` — never a stub or a fake result:

| Dependency | Resolution on this tree | Refusal |
|---|---|---|
| P4 `erev_api.controls.evidence.RunRefType` / `record_execution` (SOP-1 1b/1c; pending P2 + C1b) | absent | the lock execution refuses before any write with `ProducerMissing("erev_api.controls.evidence.record_execution …")`; CTL-016 must be recorded (`PERIOD_LOCK` run reference, the approval request id) or the lock does not happen |
| F-RPS `erev_api.domain.reports.snapshots.SNAPSHOT_DATASETS` / `SnapshotScope` | absent | `freeze_datasets` refuses by name; with the registry present, a kind of `SNAPSHOT_KINDS` missing from it refuses naming the kinds — no partial manifest |
| EDS-6 `erev_engine.stages.s15_disclosures.snapshots` (`Encoded`, `SNAPSHOT_KINDS`, `manifest_sha256`) | absent | `freeze_datasets` refuses by name (GATE-EDS preserved) |

So on `b1167410` and any tree without those producers the lock execution refuses (a `RuntimeError` subclass; the API
answers ERR-34 with nothing saved) and the request stays `PENDING` — fail closed. `PERMANENT_LOCK` needs no snapshot
and no CTL-016 row (the lock already certified) and completes when its guards hold.

### 17.1 Design

| Step | Rule and source |
|---|---|
| `request_lock(uow, state_id, body)` | `period.close` for the entity (API-R-18); lock the state row; `If-Match` on `row_version`; `gates.evaluate_gates` for the fresh results; `period_machine.decide(state, REQUEST_LOCK, Guards(comment=body.certification_comment, gate_results))` → `Refusal.problem()` (409 `invalid-transition`; 422 `validation-failed` field `certification_comment` rule BR-PLT-08; 409 `close-gates-failed` ERR-14 with one `errors[]` entry per failing gate, `rule_id` = gate code); `Accepted` → `approvals.submit(PERIOD_LOCK, subject_id = state id, summary "Lock <period> for <entity> in book <book>", comment, auto_approval=False)`; response `{approval_request_id, gate_results}` |
| `request_permanent_lock(uow, state_id, body)` | `period.close`; state `closed`; `comment` ≥ 10 (BR-PLT-08); every earlier period of the entity and book `permanently_locked` else 409 `invalid-transition` "Permanently lock <earlier> first …" (ruled Q-6); `approvals.submit(PERIOD_LOCK, …)`; response `{approval_request_id}` |
| `PERIOD_LOCK` `SubjectSpec` | `required_permission period.lock` (held by Controller only, PRD §5.6), `min_approvers 1`, `revenue_affecting False`, `content` = the period state's identity, state, `row_version` and the lock kind derived from the state (`closing` → `LOCK`, `closed` → `PERMANENT_LOCK`), `entity_id` = `period_state.entity_id`, `link_path` `/close/periods/<id>`; lifecycle registered by `close/commands.py` (`on_approved` executes; `on_rejected` / `on_voided` change nothing) |
| lock execution (`on_approved`) | lock the state; kind from the state; `period_machine.decide(LOCK or PERMANENT_LOCK, Guards(requester_id = request.preparer_id, approver_id = principal, mfa_verified_at = principal's, now, gate_results re-evaluated))` → 403 `self-approval` (BR-CLS-02), 403 `mfa-step-up-required` (BR-PLT-06 for the lock decision itself; the engine-wide change stays APR-STEPUP), 409 `close-gates-failed` (request stays `PENDING`, ruled Q-3); then, for `LOCK`: `dependencies.control_evidence()` and `dependencies.snapshot_*()` resolved first (refuse by name before any write); `freeze_datasets` → twelve `SNAPSHOT_DATASET` files; `period_state_transition` (`closing → closed`, `approval_request_id`, `period_lock_id`); `period_lock` (kind, `certification` = `certification.certification(certify(results, at))` 13 `PASSED`, `ledger_head_chain_seq` = `ledger_chain_head.last_chain_seq` of the book, `ledger_head_sha256` = its `last_seal_sha256`, `audit_head_chain_seq` / `audit_head_hmac` from `audit_chain_head`, `snapshot_manifest_sha256` = `manifest_sha256({kind: file_sha256})`, `previous_lock_id` = the period's current lock, `comment` = the certification comment); `lock_snapshot` rows (`report_run_id` NULL, ruled Q-10); `period_state.state = closed`, `current_lock_id`; `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED` item `PASSED` (`gates.store_results`); reconciliations `REVIEWED` / `AUTO_CERTIFIED` → `CERTIFIED` with `certified_at` (see Q-12 for `period_lock_id`); next period `future → open` (BR-CLS-03); CTL-016 `record_execution(control_id CTL-016, run_ref_type PERIOD_LOCK, run_ref_id = approval request id, population 13, exceptions 0, PASS, detail {gates}, entity, book, period)`; NTF-06 `PERIOD_LOCKED` to Controllers, Revenue Reviewers and Auditors (role holders); audit `period.lock`. For `PERMANENT_LOCK`: transition `closed → permanently_locked`, `period_lock` kind `PERMANENT_LOCK` with the current gate results as `certification`, no snapshots, audit `period.permanent_lock` |
| `queries.list_locks(session, state_id)` | T-CLS-04 rows of the period newest first: id, kind, created_at, created_by, reason_code, comment, ledger_head_chain_seq, snapshot_manifest_sha256, previous_lock_id, diff_report_file_id, approval_request_id |
| `snapshots.freeze_datasets(uow, entity_id, book_code, period_id, known_at, *, registry=None)` | resolve the engine kinds and the F-RPS registry (refuse by name); every kind present else refuse naming the missing kinds; for each kind `Encoded = registry[kind](uow, SnapshotScope(entity_id, book_code, period_id, known_at))`; `files.store_file(purpose SNAPSHOT_DATASET, csv bytes, "<kind>.csv", text/csv)`; `DatasetFile(kind, file_id, file_sha256, row_count, control_totals)`; `manifest_of(datasets)` = the engine's `manifest_sha256`; `write_lock_snapshots(uow, period_lock_id, datasets)` inserts T-CLS-05 rows |

### 17.2 The twelve acceptance tests (BUILD_SPEC :8117)

**Record correction (Codex CLO-6 review R2, 2026-09-20; verified by the supervisor).** As first committed in this
docs-first section (`efa7a782`, before any test existed) and at Codex's runtime `c6620c9d`, this paragraph said the
twelve tests were "all written". That was wrong at those heads: at `c6620c9d` the only tests added by CLO-6 were the two
unit modules `tests/unit/close/test_dependencies.py` (4) and `tests/unit/close/test_snapshots_freeze.py` (5); none of the
twelve specified functions existed at their stated paths. The twelve scenarios (plus `test_checklist_items_carry_the_certified_result`)
were written in commit `f4e892d3`: `tests/domain/close/test_lock.py` (10 functions), `tests/api/test_lock_api.py` (2) and
`tests/pg/test_db_invariants.py::test_ctl_015_after_lock_inserts_refused`; they exist from `f4e892d3` (and `0ac06439`) on,
collected, DB-bound, NOT RUN (databases not provisioned). The supporting CLO-6 locking world `worlds.k01_pellworth(uow,
through=date)` named by BUILD_SPEC is **owed**, not built: the tests use the existing report-world helper
`worlds.k01_pellworth(app, keyring, clock, files, through=…)` (L6-3-Q-17) and the CloseWorld personas. Rule for this
record from here on: a test is called "written" only when the function exists at the stated path in the same tree as the
statement, naming the commit. The retest target for R2 is any head ≥ `0ac06439`.

All DB-bound; NOT RUN here (databases not provisioned; measured by the integrated batch on merged main). On a tree
without the P4 and F-RPS producers the lock-executing tests refuse by name and cannot pass: they are honest acceptance
tests of the finished path, not of this tree. CPU-side: `tests/unit/close/test_dependencies.py` (each producer is
absent on this tree and the refusal names it), `tests/unit/close/test_snapshots_freeze.py` (a registry missing kinds
refuses naming them before any write; the engine module absent refuses by name), `tests/unit/close/test_period_machine.py`
already covers the request and execution decisions.

## 18 CLO-5 correction set (Codex composition review `PRODUCTION-F-CLO-CLO5-REVIEW-b116741.md`; runtime `0621c715`, record `b1167410`)

Codex's native composition harness (seven cases over an inert session: `evaluate → raise_exception_item →
data_quality_blocking → data_quality_gate`; not a DB run) passed 4 and failed 3, reproducing two defects that let
`DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR` pass despite a current `BLOCKING` finding. The original evidence of §16 stands as written; this
section appends the rulings and the corrections.

| Finding | Defect | Ruling | Correction |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** current scope lost on deduplication | the dedupe key `DATA_QUALITY:<code>:<subject>` carried no entity or period; the FX subject no entity; the shared existing-item and upsert paths bump `occurrence_count` on the FIRST item and keep its entity / period; the gate counts only the current entity / period → a September recurrence of an August duplicate, or entity B's missing EUR→USD closing rate after entity A's, left the current period's gate `PASSED` | **D-98 57**: an item's identity carries its affected scope (tenant, source, code, subject, entity, period, and contract where the subject is contract-scoped; the FX subject gains entity); a recurrence in another scope is a distinct unresolved item; stored scopes are never overwritten; the gate derives from each item's own scope. Widening the identity does not touch the admitted Q-8 source key (`ux_source_invoice__external` is the source table's key, not the exception's), so the single-item alternative with an occurrence table is not needed | `monitor_rules.MonitorFinding` carries `entity_id` and `period_id`; `dedupe_key = DATA_QUALITY:<code>:<entity_id>:<period_id>:<subject>` (04 T-IMP-05 rev 1.22); `evaluate(..., entity_id, period_id)`; `monitors.run_monitors` passes the scope; the shared helper's existing-item and upsert paths keep `entity_id`, `period_id`, `contract_id` untouched (they never set them); `gates.data_quality_blocking` counts by the item's own entity and period |
| **R2** severity escalation ignored for an existing item | the same inactive contract first `WARNING`, then an effective `ERROR` override: evaluation emitted `BLOCKING`, but the existing-item path returned before applying it → stored `WARNING`, gate `PASSED` | **D-98 58**: an existing unresolved item re-evaluated at a different effective severity takes the current severity with an appended, auditable severity-history entry (from, to, evaluated_at, cause); the gate reads the current severity; a lowered severity follows the same rule; status never changes automatically | `imports.exceptions._seen_again(uow, key, severity=…)` compares the stored severity with the current finding's, updates `severity` (T-IMP-05 has no DB-03 column allow-list; IM-M) and writes the audit event `exception_item.severity_changed` with `before {severity}` / `after {severity, evaluated_at, cause: dedupe key}`; `RaisedItem` gains `severity_changed` and `previous_severity`; the concurrent-insert upsert branch: ~~deferred to the next run~~ — withdrawn by supervisor ruling D-98 69 and reconciled in the same transaction (§21) |
| **R3** source-predicted DB test setup failures | `test_dq_duplicate_invoice` inserted the same (tenant, system, external id, version) twice against `ux_source_invoice__external`; the unbilled threshold cases inserted a one-line unsealed posting, and the fixture path commits before `_run` so 0040's deferred seal constraint fires | none needed (test scaffolding) | the duplicate test uses the admitted identity-tuple duplicate (distinct external ids, equal customer / number / amount / date; Q-8 rule 2) and its wording follows; the unbilled cases post a balanced two-line posting sealed with `ledger_seal_values` — the 61 / 60-day oracle unchanged. Still DB-bound, collected, NOT RUN (no lane database; no `CREATE DATABASE`) |
| **R4** documentation | 04 §14.3 rev 1.21 left AUTO-BOOTSTRAP's conditions sentence attached to the new `DQ-SYSTEM` bullet; §16.1 described the planned `fx_layer_movement` collector as actual | none | 04 rev 1.22 moves the displaced tail back to its bullet; §16.1's `DQ_NEGATIVE_LIABILITY_LAYER` row now states the interim aggregate source and that offsetting layers stay undetected by it |

Positive controls kept blocking (tests below): a same-scope open repeat counts the same item again; an `IN_PROGRESS`
(reopened) item counts again; a fresh `BLOCKING` finding after the earlier item was `RESOLVED` or `WAIVED` creates a new
open item (the partial unique index covers open statuses only). Nothing resolves automatically (D-98 55).

Acceptance stays open: CLO-19 `EXCEPTION_CHECK`, CLO-6/7, P4's durable control execution, F-RPS dataset completeness
and GATE-EDS are separate.

## 19 CLO-6 correction slice (Codex `PRODUCTION-F-CLO-CLO6-REVIEW-c6620c9.md`; runtime `c6620c9d`; rulings D-98 61, 63, 64)

Eleven agreed controls: 10 pass, 1 failed (the emitted-order oracle); source and native evidence, not an observed
PostgreSQL error or a successful lock. Order of this slice: the R2 record correction first (`ef93f7ea`, §17.2), then
these corrections.

| Finding / question | Ruling | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| **R1** immediate FK references precede the parent INSERT: the permanent-lock helper (and the ordinary lock in the same source order) emitted the `period_state_transition` INSERT naming the new lock id and the `period_state.current_lock_id` UPDATE before the `period_lock` INSERT; 0047 defers only `period_lock → transition`, while `transition → lock` and `current_lock → lock` are immediate keys | **D-98 61**: allocate both ids up front; INSERT `period_lock` first (carrying its already-deferred transition edge), then the transition INSERT and the `current_lock_id` UPDATE that reference it; the same order in the ordinary lock; transition / audit behaviour and soft close preserved; "before any write" means before any snapshot or lock write — gate / checklist and approval statements that precede a refusal rely on same-UOW rollback, to be proved by a test | `commands._persist_lock(uow, scope, *, kind, transition_id, lock_id, …)` shared by both lock kinds: `period_lock` INSERT (with the pre-allocated `period_state_transition_id`), then `_record_transition(transition_id=…, period_lock_id=lock_id, set_current_lock=True)`; `_record_transition` accepts the pre-allocated id; unit oracle `tests/unit/close/test_lock_write_order.py` over an inert statement-capturing session (the index of the `period_lock` INSERT precedes every statement that references it) — fail-first on the current order; DB test `test_refused_lock_persists_nothing` (NOT RUN) for the same-UOW rollback |
| **R2** record integrity | supervisor: name `f4e892d3` as the commit where the twelve (plus one) tests were written; never call a test written before it exists at its path in the same tree | `ef93f7ea` (§17.2, §17.2b) |
| **Q-12** `reconciliation.period_lock_id` at lock | **D-98 63**: T-CLS-06 gains `period_lock_id` in its updatable list as a write-once member — set at lock certification only while NULL, never changed afterwards (a second write is a refusal, not an overwrite); docs first (04 T-CLS-06 rev 1.23 + the transition rule); `test_lock_certifies_reconciliations` keeps asserting it | `db/transitions.py` `"reconciliation"`: `period_lock_id` in `updatable_columns` and `set_once`; migration `0055_clo_6_reconciliation_period_lock_id.py`: `GRANT UPDATE (period_lock_id)` to `erev_app` and `CREATE OR REPLACE` of `tg_reconciliation__transition` with the re-rendered DB-03 body (downgrade restores the 0047 body and revokes the grant); `_certify_reconciliations` sets `period_lock_id` with `certified_at`; the pins `tests/pg/test_migrations.py::test_single_head` and `tests/api/test_me.py` move to `0055`; a CPU test pins the frozen body to `transition_trigger_sql("reconciliation")` (the DG-ARC-07 rule without a database) |
| **Q-13** request / approve permissions | **D-98 64**: confirmed as applied — `period.close` requests the lock or permanent lock, `period.lock` decides; the requester is never the approver (BR-CLS-02 403 `self-approval`); the permanent lock needs a second Controller | none (already so) |

Noted, not accepted by Codex (unchanged here): the dependency resolvers refuse by name; the default freeze and the
six-missing-kind preflight refuse before the UOW is touched; the manifest controls use synthetic kinds and hashes with an
engine callback, not actual EDS output or stored datasets. Q-12's lock linkage, `JE_COMPLETE`, real snapshots, CTL-015 /

the INSERT boundary a supplied same-scope OPEN `WARNING` row wins the partial-unique conflict identity; the `ON CONFLICT` SET
changed only `occurrence_count`, `last_seen_at`, `updated_at`, `row_version`, and the helper returned the winner without
applying the current `BLOCKING` severity or any history — stored `WARNING` / occurrence 2, `severity_changed False`, no
severity audit; `data_quality_blocking` → 0 → `DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR` `PASSED` against the 1 / `FAILED` oracle. The BLOCKING
winner and the ordinary new ERROR were correct. §18 R2 and the "Not changed" paragraph had deferred that branch to the next
run; **D-98 69** rules the reconciliation into the same transaction, before returning and before gating, and the deferral
text leaves the record (struck above with a pointer here).

| Commit | Content |
|---|---|
| `e1691a17` | docs first: 04 rev 1.24 (provisional; renumbered at merge prep with 1.22 / 1.23 → 1.30 / 1.31) — T-IMP-05 `dedupe_key` note: the upsert winner is reconciled in the same transaction (one occurrence increment, current effective severity when it differs, one audited `exception_item.severity_changed` event with the actual prior / current severities and the row version written; scope and status untouched) |
| `d760a5fa` | `imports/exceptions.py`: the upsert's RETURNING gains `severity` and `row_version`; when the returned id is not the candidate's, `_reconcile_winner` computes `severity_change(row.severity, current)` and, when it differs, issues one UPDATE keyed on `(tenant_id, id, row_version = returned)` setting `severity`, SC-M stamps and `row_version + 1`, then records the history event at the written version through the shared `_record_severity_change` (also used by `_seen_again`); `RaisedItem.severity_changed` / `previous_severity` are set on that branch; no second `_seen_again` call, so exactly one occurrence increment. Lowering and unchanged-severity behaviour, the gate oracle and every other producer unchanged. Test `tests/unit/close/test_conflict_severity.py` (3): Codex's three projections over an inert session that applies only the emitted `ON CONFLICT` SET / RETURNING columns and an UPDATE only where its WHERE names the winner's id and the returned row version; asserts the stored row (id, status, scope, occurrence 2, severity `BLOCKING`, row_version 3), the returned `RaisedItem`, exactly one history event (`before {severity: WARNING}`, `after {severity: BLOCKING, from, to, evaluated_at, cause}`, `object_version "3"`), the statement shape `Select, Insert, Update` (no retry), and the composed gate `FAILED` / 1; the BLOCKING winner (no history, `Select, Insert`) and the new ERROR (create audit) controls |

### 22.5 CLO-7a-fix-2 (`18b1388d`): entity / book columns in the key table

Where a kind's dataset header carries `entity_code` and / or a book column (or the report's equivalent), they are part of that kind's `KeySpec.columns` (§15.2.7 lists entity and book; Codex reads the table literally): `JE_POPULATION` → (`entity_code`, `book`, `je_no`, `line_no`); `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_REGISTER` → (`entity_code`, `adjustment_no`); `WATERFALL`, `CONTRACT_BALANCES`, `RPO`, `PRIOR_PERIOD_POB_REVENUE` already carried `entity_code`. The kinds whose header carries neither (`CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD`, `RPO_ROLLFORWARD`, `DISAGGREGATION`, `COST_ROLLFORWARD`, `OUT_OF_PERIOD_REGISTER`, `MODIFICATION_REGISTER`) keep their entries, the provenance note saying so; no kind's header carries a book column except `je_population`'s `book`. The completeness test requires every entity / book column of a kind's fixture header to be in its key and the note otherwise; the duplicate-key fixture carries the JE key columns. No governed document changed. Captured statuses: `make fmt` rc=0, `make lint` rc=0, `make typecheck` rc=0, focused `test_relock_diff.py` rc=0 (6 passed), `tests/unit/close` + `tests/unit/approvals` + `tests/architecture` rc=0 (234 passed).

### 22.8 CLO-7d declared measures (Codex CLO7-ATTR-R1 on `4d8e875b`; supervisor ruling D-98 87) — docs `8b9d4342`, code `d9900701`

Codex's CLO-7c source review confirmed the event-key and code-column corrections and found that `_column_change` still classified by value shape: both-decimal values became a measure with a delta, so a `CONTRACT_BALANCES` row whose amounts are unchanged but whose `customer_name` moves from "123" to "456" would have been a measure change with delta 333.

**Docs first (`8b9d4342`, no new rows).** ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-20a and its provisional 1.14 log row gain the declared-measure sentence (classification by each kind's declared money / quantity columns per the §15.2.7 control totals and the builder's shape; every other non-key column an attribute whatever its values look like; a declared measure with a non-decimal value refuses by name; an empty cell is an absent value; numeric-looking names never rejected or rewritten); 04 T-CLS-05 "Dataset identity" note and the provisional 1.27 log row amended the same way. No new revision row was necessary: both statements sit in the CLO-7c rows still awaiting their merge-prep numbers (1.23 / 1.39).

Scope kept to the framework branch and the source contract the builders read; other lanes' builders untouched (ENG-C4's RPT-05 and ENG-C6's two keep their refusals until their next slices).

### 25.1 Docs first (`8b1655b7`)

| Doc | Revision | Change |
|---|---|---|
| ENGINE_SPEC_B | **1.31** (reserved; 1.27–1.30 other lanes) | S15-R-19 branch statement: a run given `period_lock_id` reads the immutable `lock_snapshot` dataset of its report's E-64 kind and no live table — CSV = the frozen bytes (`output_sha256` = `file_sha256`, `row_count` / `control_totals` the snapshot's), JSON / XLSX / PDF from the frozen rows as text-kind columns with the S15-R-18 row key; refusals by name for a report without a lock dataset kind, a lock without that kind's snapshot, a hash mismatch (S15-INV-06), a missing row-key column; a run without `period_lock_id` unchanged; no cell explanation on an as-locked run. Header + log row. |
| SCREENS_B | **1.17** (reserved; 1.14–1.16 other lanes) | §0.5 RV-04 run semantics (as-locked reads the frozen dataset; CSV = the frozen file; grid / XLSX / PDF from the frozen rows; the named refusal on RV-14 instead of current figures under an "as locked" label; no cell explainer). Correction inside the same unreleased revision (carried in `391a52b2`): the S15-R-19 missing-column clause first named "a row-key column of S15-R-20a"; the frozen datasets are the engine's S15-R-18 encoding, whose first column IS the `row_key` (sort key), so the clause now names "the S15-R-18 `row_key` column". The 1.31 log row ("a missing row-key column") needed no change.

### 25.2 Code (`391a52b2`)

`backend/erev_api/domain/reports/locked.py` (new, 199 lines):

- `SNAPSHOT_KIND_BY_REPORT` — the §15.2.7 kind ↔ report map inverted (twelve codes ↔ twelve E-64 kinds); `snapshot_kind_of(code)` → kind or None.
- `locked_dataset(uow, report_code=, lock_id=)` — refuses by name before any read when the report has no kind (`NO_DATASET`, names the code); reads the `lock_snapshot` row of (lock, kind) (`SNAPSHOT_MISSING` names lock, kind and code); reads the stored file back through `files.store.open_file`; `verify` refuses when the bytes do not hash to `file_sha256` (`HASH_MISMATCH`, cites S15-INV-06) or the row count differs (`ROW_COUNT_MISMATCH`).
- `report_data(dataset)` — the frozen rows as `ReportData`: every header except `row_key` a text-kind column (key = header = the engine column key), each row keyed by the dataset's own `row_key` cell (`KEY_MISSING` names the column when the header lacks it), the snapshot's control totals, no tie-outs (the freeze evaluated them). `csv_bytes(dataset)` — the frozen bytes and `file_sha256`.
- `LockedRefusal(Problem)` — 422 `validation-failed`, one error on `parameters.period_lock_id` with rule `S15-R-19`; message = detail.

`framework.py`: `run_report` branches on `run["period_lock_id"]` — set: `locked.locked_dataset` + `locked.report_data`, rendered by the new `_render_locked` (CSV: content = the frozen bytes, `sha256` = the snapshot's `file_sha256`, RV-02 manifest over those bytes; JSON / XLSX / PDF: the ordinary `_render` over the frozen rows); the builder is not called and `NOT_AVAILABLE` no longer fires for a locked run of a report without a builder. Unset: unchanged (builder, `_render`). `explain_cell` refuses an as-locked run by name (`not-found`, `EXPLAIN_LOCKED`: cell explanation reads live contributors) before looking up the explainer. Layering: `reports` imports nothing new from `close` (the earlier `relock_diff.KEY_COLUMNS` idea was dropped for the `row_key` column); `tests/architecture` green.

**Catalogue fact, pinned by name and NOT fixed here:** `modification_register` and `manual_adjustment_register` do not accept `period_lock_id` in their T-RPT-01 definitions (the other ten dataset reports do), so an as-locked run of those two registers is unreachable until their owners add the parameter (`test_two_registers_do_not_yet_accept_the_lock_parameter`). Scenarios: (1) a run with `period_lock_id` reads the frozen file — the live builder (replaced by one that raises) is never called; K01 liability 29,944.11 as frozen text; (2) CSV output = the frozen file (`output.sha256` = `file_sha256`; the download's bytes equal; a rerun reproduces); (3) a 5,000.00 billing appended to SF-ORD-10001 after the lock moves the live September balances while the as-locked run returns the frozen figures; (4) `revenue_from_opening_liability` (no dataset kind) and `contract_balances` against a lock without a CONTRACT_BALANCES snapshot each end FAILED after RV-14's second attempt with the S15-R-19 refusal naming report / kind / lock; (5) a current run after the lock still calls the builder and shows live figures (frozen liability of 1.00 never appears); (6) `GET /explain/report-runs/{id}/cell` on an as-locked run → 404 with the named message.

### 25.5 CLO8-SCOPE-R1 — the scope of an as-locked run is the lock's (Codex packet 1554, source hold on CLO-8) — docs `38e5fe24`, code `a4eecb30`

Finding (Codex, source-composed, no observed leak): `_resolve` compared the lock's entity only with ALL of the preparer's permitted entities, so a preparer allowed A + B could request `entity_codes=[B]` with A's lock; the run persisted B's scope with A's lock, the locked branch returned A's entire frozen dataset labelled B, and `visible` / `run_row` then authorized any same-tenant B-only reader (`report.run`, `report.export`) to list, detail, read the JSON rows and download the output. Book and period selectors were not checked against the lock either.

Governed rule (docs first, in place under the unlanded rows — `38e5fe24`): ENGINE_SPEC_B **1.31** S15-R-19, SCREENS_B **1.17** RV-04, 04 **1.53** T-RPT-02 (log rows extended in the same revisions): the scope of an as-locked run IS the lock's — one entity, its book, its period. Explicit `entity_codes`, `book`, `period_key` / `from_period_key` / `to_period_key` must equal them or the run is refused by name at creation; omitted selectors are derived from the lock; no other parameter is admitted (the frozen dataset carries no filter, dimension, view, time-band or as-of choice — each refused by name); the run row persists the lock's `entity_ids = [period_lock.entity_id]`, `book_code` and selector parameters; the job reconciles the persisted scope with the lock's before reading and refuses by name; API-R-41 visibility of an as-locked run additionally requires `entity_ids = ARRAY[period_lock.entity_id]`, so readers are authorized against the real frozen scope and an inconsistent as-locked run is served to no one. An entire frozen dataset never answers under a different requested scope or filter.

Code (`a4eecb30`), the four fix items of the dispatch:

| # | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| (1) creation | `locked.LockScope` / `lock_scope` (lock → entity code, book, period key); `locked.reconcile_selectors(given, scope, properties, kind=)`; `framework._resolve` | Mismatched explicit selectors refused by name (`ENTITY_MISMATCH` on `parameters.entity_codes`, `BOOK_MISMATCH`, `PERIOD_MISMATCH` per period key); any other given key refused by name (`NOT_A_LOCK_SELECTOR`); persisted parameters = the lock, `known_at`, and the lock-derived selectors the report defines; `entity_ids = (lock entity,)`; `book_code` the lock's. A report without a lock dataset (`snapshot_kind_of` None) is now refused at creation (`NO_DATASET`, rule S15-R-19) — no run persisted. |
| (2) execution | `locked.run_mismatch(run, scope)` → first differing field or None; `locked.assert_run_matches(session, run)`; `run_report` locked branch | Before `locked_dataset`: the lock exists (`RUN_LOCK_MISSING`) and the persisted `period_lock_id`, `entity_ids`, `book_code`, parameter keys and selectors equal the lock's (`RUN_SCOPE_INCONSISTENT`); refusal by name → run FAILED with the problem. |
| (3) downstream | `framework._scoped` (the former `visible`) + `_consistent_with_lock()`; `visible` = both; `run_row` → `_refuse_inconsistent_lock_run` | Lists, detail, `/data`, `/output`, rerun and `explain_cell` all pass through `visible` / `run_row`: an as-locked run is visible only while `entity_ids = ARRAY[(SELECT period_lock.entity_id …)]`; a hidden inconsistent run within the caller's plain scope is refused by name (`not-found` + `RUN_SCOPE_INCONSISTENT`) rather than reported absent; a run outside scope stays a plain not-found. |
| (4) existing inconsistent runs | the same three | Stated, not claimed to exist: hidden from every list; refused by name when addressed by any reader (including one whose scope covers the mislabelled entity — the message names the run number only, never the frozen entity); FAILED by name if still QUEUED when the job runs; a SUCCEEDED one keeps its rows on disk but no route serves them; remedy is a new run. No migration, no data change. |

Tests — fail-first captured: `pytest -q tests/unit/reports/test_locked_source.py` **rc=2 before** (`AttributeError: module 'erev_api.domain.reports.locked' has no attribute 'LockScope'` — `.run/f-clo/failfirst/clo8s-unit-before.log`), **rc=0 after** (`27 passed` — `clo8s-unit-after.log`). Nine CPU cases added (matching selectors accepted and normalised; omitted selectors derived for a single-period and a range report; another entity / both entities refused by name; another book, another period, a mismatched range start refused by name; `contract_external_id` / `include_zero` / `currency_view` / `as_of` refused by name; `run_mismatch` per field; the compiled `visible` predicate carries the scope containment, the NULL-lock branch and `entity_ids = ARRAY[(SELECT erev.period_lock.entity_id …)]`; `run_row` refuses a hidden inconsistent run by name in two statements and leaves an out-of-scope run a plain not-found). Four DB-bound scenarios added to `tests/domain/reports/test_as_locked.py` (**collected, NOT RUN — no lane chain (databases not provisioned)**; a second entity AVM-UK on AVM-US's calendar; a UK-only Revenue Accountant via `holding(..., entity_ids=[uk])`): Maya (AVM-US + AVM-UK) with a valid AVM-US lock requesting AVM-UK / both / IFRS15 / FY2026-P08 / a contract filter → 422 by name, no run persisted; same-scope control unchanged for explicit and omitted selectors (frozen bytes, `output.sha256` = `file_sha256`, persisted parameters exactly the lock-derived four + `known_at`, `entity_scope` = AVM-US); the UK-only reader cannot list, detail, read data, download or explain an AVM-US as-locked run while Maya still can; a hand-made inconsistent QUEUED run (scope rewritten to AVM-UK behind the framework) is hidden from both readers' lists, refused by name to both, and FAILS by name when the job runs. The unsupported-source scenario moved to creation time (422); the lock-without-snapshot scenario still fails at execution. The six earlier matching-scope cases are unchanged.

Correction to the commit message of `a4eecb30`, stated plainly: it says "+10 CPU … then 28 passed"; the tree holds **nine** added CPU tests and the captured after-run is **27 passed** (18 + 9). The count in this record is the one to rely on.

Captured statuses at `a4eecb30`: `make fmt` rc=0; `make lint` rc=0 (one F811 — the `report_run` table import shadowed by the `report_run` helper import in the DB test — fixed by aliasing the table `report_run_table` before the commit); `make typecheck` rc=0; unit `test_locked_source.py` rc=0 (27 passed); focused set `tests/unit/reports tests/unit/test_governed_docs_revisions.py tests/unit/close tests/architecture` rc=0 (**297 passed**); collection `tests/domain/reports` rc=0 (52 collected, was 48). Both commits made only inside the lint AND typecheck AND focused guard. CLO-8b unchanged and separate.


### 25.6 Codex packet 1558 (CLO8-R1) attached — the same finding as 1554's CLO8-SCOPE-R1, one hold; boundaries carried

Attached to the §25.5 fix (team lead, after `cf370207`): `PRODUCTION-F-CLO8-SOURCE-REVIEW-39c5c359.md` SHA256 `d83e4f359be37212f7aebd5ddb5e404ba0c122838f2d9929616171b3c3dc419b`; manifest `source-observations/f-clo8-39c5c359/manifest.json` SHA256 `27f9337d993e5a4319f2ad454210514e8206a5820209fd744289071d5e2aed81`; 17 artifacts; pins record `39c5c359` / runtime `391a52b2` / docs `8b1655b7`. Its label CLO8-R1 names the SAME finding as 1554's CLO8-SCOPE-R1 — one hold, not a second issue; no new finding.

Confirmed at source by the review: the frozen hash and row-count checks, the exact CSV bytes, no live-builder fallback for data, the locked-cell-explanation refusal.

Boundaries, stated in substance as the review states them:

- "No live data" refers to dataset SOURCING. Ordinary permission, definition and manifest metadata reads still happen on an as-locked run (the run row, the definition row, the lock row, the `lock_snapshot` row, the file object, the release and setting reads for the stamp). The claim is that no live REPORT table is read for the figures — not that the run touches no table.
- The 18 CPU tests of `391a52b2` (27 at `a4eecb30`) are meaningful at their helper boundaries — `locked.verify` / `report_data` / `csv_bytes` / `locked_dataset` with a session double, `_render_locked` with `_run_stamp` replaced, `explain_cell` and `run_row` with `run_row` / the session replaced. They do not run the job, the API or the database.
- The six DB-bound cases of `391a52b2` remain collected / NOT RUN, use MATCHING scope, and construct the lock through the domain's private writers (`_record_transition`, `_persist_lock`) because CLO-10 blocks the public `request_lock` path. They therefore neither measure the complete public lock workflow nor — on their own — close the scope mismatch. The four cases of `a4eecb30` address the scope mismatch by name (mismatched selectors at creation, the UK-only reader, the hand-made inconsistent run) but share the private-writer construction and the NOT RUN status; the public lock workflow stays a CLO-10 / integrated-batch matter.
- CLO-8b (the two registers' `period_lock_id` catalogue rows) stays separate.

Governed basis of the §25.5 scope normalization and checks, as directed: 04 T-CLS-05 — `ux_lock_snapshot (tenant_id, period_lock_id, snapshot_kind)`, one frozen dataset per lock and kind, where a lock (T-CLS-04) is one entity, one book, one period; SCREENS_B RV-04 — the `snapshot` value on report screens is "the `period_lock.id` of the context entity, book and period (OQ-B-04)", passed as `period_lock_id`. `locked.LockScope` is exactly that partition (lock → `entity_id` / `entity_code`, `book_code`, `period_id` / `period_key`), `reconcile_selectors` normalizes and checks the run's `entity_codes`, `book` and period selectors against it, `run_mismatch` reconciles the persisted row against it, and `_consistent_with_lock` authorizes readers against the lock's `entity_id`. No code change was needed to adopt the basis; this section records the citation. Tree unchanged from `cf370207` apart from this record.


### 25.7 CLO8-SCOPE-R1 residual (Codex packet 1622 item 1) — FULL scope consistency on the list and on every read — docs `29260ed5`, code `10a1033c`

Codex's verdict on `1b7137c3`: PARTIALLY corrected (`PRODUCTION-F-CLO8-SCOPE-REVIEW-1b7137c3.md` SHA256 `0e8337b970facf652e7d160d9a33e26e549aeae2ccb648556437212db2fbae59`; manifest `ec762625a6caef900386530a0871dfa2a2b3f678def425e76aae944c12f954ba`; 15 artifacts). Closed there: creation derives the lock scope, compares the original explicit selectors and rejects unsupported filters; omitted selectors reach normalization; the worker invokes the full matcher before frozen data; the A-lock / B-reader disclosure path is closed; no numerical / oracle change; frozen-reader ASTs and engine tree preserved. Remaining (same hold): `_consistent_with_lock` checked ONLY entity ids; `run_row` returned an admitted row without full reconciliation; completed output reads opened the stored files directly; the worker's SUCCEEDED fast path preceded its check — so a historically COMPLETED same-entity run with book IFRS15, period FY2026-P08 or a formerly admitted contract filter (schema-valid choices the pre-fix resolver admitted independently of the lock) would still have been listed / read / exported under inconsistent metadata, against §25.5's promise. No such row is claimed to exist.

Docs first (`29260ed5`, in place under the unlanded rows): ENGINE_SPEC_B **1.31** S15-R-19, SCREENS_B **1.17** RV-04, 04 **1.53** T-RPT-02 now say the consistency that hides or refuses is the FULL scope — `entity_ids` = the lock's entity, `book_code` = the lock's book, every persisted period selector = the lock's period, no parameter outside the admitted set, `entity_codes` = the lock's entity code — evaluated by the list predicate and again on every addressed read of a run in ANY status (detail, rows, output, manifest, rerun, cell explanation), completed runs included, never dependent on another worker execution.

Code (`10a1033c`):

| Where | What |
|---|---|
| `framework._consistent_with_lock` | The SQL twin of `locked.run_mismatch`: `period_lock_id IS NULL OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM period_lock ⋈ legal_entity ⋈ period WHERE period_lock.id = report_run.period_lock_id AND report_run.entity_ids = ARRAY[period_lock.entity_id] AND report_run.book_code = period_lock.book_code AND report_run.parameters − ARRAY[admitted keys] = '{}'::jsonb AND (parameters->'entity_codes' IS NULL OR = jsonb_build_array(legal_entity.code)) AND (parameters->>'book' IS NULL OR = period_lock.book_code) AND, per period selector, (parameters->>key IS NULL OR = period.period_key))`. Used by `visible`, hence by the list, `run_row` and everything behind it. The permission / entity containment (`_scoped`) is retained unchanged. |
| `framework.run_row` | After the predicate admits a row with `period_lock_id`: `locked.lock_scope` + `locked.run_mismatch(dict(row), scope)` in Python — the authoritative reconciliation on EVERY addressed read (detail, `/data`, `/output`, `?part=manifest`, rerun, cell explanation), whatever the row's status; refusal by name as `not-found` with `RUN_SCOPE_INCONSISTENT` (or `RUN_LOCK_MISSING` when the lock is gone). Never deferred to a worker. The §25.5 by-name path for a row the predicate hid (`_refuse_inconsistent_lock_run`) stays. |
| `framework.run_report` | `locked.assert_run_matches` moved before ANY path, the SUCCEEDED fast path included (kept as well before the dataset read). |

Handling of existing inconsistent completed rows, stated: hidden from every list by the predicate; refused by name on every addressed read by `run_row`; their stored output files stay on disk but no report route serves them (the generic `/files` routes are separate and unchanged); the worker yields no outcome for them; remedy is a new run under the rule. No migration, no data change.

Tests — fail-first captured: `pytest -q tests/unit/reports/test_locked_source.py` **rc=1 before** (`clo8t-unit-before.log`: `EXISTS (SELECT` absent from the compiled predicate; the admitted keys not bound; `run_row` DID NOT RAISE on a wrong-book row), **rc=0 after** (`32 passed` — `clo8t-unit-after.log`). Five CPU cases added: the compiled `visible` predicate carries the EXISTS, entity, book, per-period-selector, admitted-key subtraction and `jsonb_build_array(legal_entity.code)` clauses; the bound literals include exactly `ADMITTED_KEYS` / `PERIOD_KEYS` and not `contract_external_id`; `run_row` refuses a RETURNED as-locked row with a wrong `book_code`, a wrong `period_key`, a formerly admitted `contract_external_id` or a wrong `book` parameter by name in two statements; serves a consistent as-locked row (two statements) and a current row (one); refuses a row whose lock is gone (`RUN_LOCK_MISSING`). The §25.5 entity-only SQL assertion was updated to the EXISTS rendering. One DB-bound scenario added to `tests/domain/reports/test_as_locked.py` (**collected, NOT RUN — no lane chain (databases not provisioned)**; the file now holds 11): three SUCCEEDED copies of a real as-locked run (book IFRS15 with the `book` parameter; period FY2026-P08; the K01 contract filter), inserted directly because a finished T-RPT-02 row is immutable (DB-03) — absent from Maya's list; detail, `/data`, `/output`, `?part=manifest`, the cell explainer and `rerun` each 404 by name (`RUN_SCOPE_INCONSISTENT` with the copy's run number); the matching completed control is served with its frozen figures (K01 liability 29,944.11 as frozen text). Producer counts attributed: the 4 + 6 earlier DB cases stay NOT RUN by name; the six original cases use matching scope and private lock writers (§25.6).

### 25.8 Codex packet 1649 item 1 — hold KEPT at `27e598dd`; two bounded source corrections — code `2a6eb20b` (docs unchanged)

Codex's review of `27e598dd` (runtime `10a1033c`; `PRODUCTION-F-CLO8-COMPLETED-SCOPE-REVIEW-27e598dd.md`, SHA256 prefix `a893f254`) confirms the full-scope checks structurally cover completed wrong-book / wrong-period / extra-filter runs, the addressed reads and the SUCCEEDED fast path, and keeps the hold for two concrete source defects. No governed rule sentence needed to change; docs untouched.

| # | Defect (Codex, source-predicted, not observed) | Correction (`2a6eb20b`) |
|---|---|---|
| (a) | `framework._consistent_with_lock` compared `parameters ->> 'book'` (PostgreSQL text) directly with `period_lock.book_code`, a native `erev.book_code` ENUM, with no cast — under ordinary operator resolution a type-resolution error raised before any Python revalidation, hitting EVERY query through `visible` (consistent and current rows alike; the NULL-lock OR branch does not bypass SQL type checking). | The trusted enum column is cast to text — `cast(period_lock.c.book_code, Text)` — never the stored JSON into the enum (a malformed value would raise). Every other scope check unchanged. The compiled-query unit assertion now requires `= CAST(erev.period_lock.book_code AS TEXT)` and forbids any cast into `book_code`. Targeted DB acceptance of the predicate remains necessary and is **NOT RUN in the lane (no database)**. |
| (b) | `test_as_locked.py` derived each completed copy's `report_run_no` from the model's suffix plus `len(changes)`; the period and filter copies pass one change each, so the third insert collided on migration 0048's `ux_report_run__no (tenant_id, report_run_no)` before the access assertions ran. | `_copy_number(model_no, changes)` appends the first six hex characters of the SHA-256 of the sorted JSON of `changes` — deterministic and distinct per copy; the three copies are built from one `COPY_CHANGES` table merged over the stored parameters, with an in-scenario distinctness assertion. The unique constraint, the scope mutations (book IFRS15 + `book` parameter; period FY2026-P08; the K01 contract filter), the by-name refusal assertions and the frozen 29,944.11 control are unchanged. |

Fail-first, captured: `pytest -q tests/unit/reports/test_locked_source.py tests/domain/reports/test_as_locked.py::test_completed_copy_numbers_are_distinct` **rc=1 before** (`.run/f-clo/failfirst/clo8u-before.log`: the cast assertion red — the compiled predicate carried `= erev.period_lock.book_code` without a cast; the copy numbers collided exactly as predicted — `['RPT-S000122', 'RPT-S000121', 'RPT-S000121']`), **rc=0 after** (`clo8u-after.log`: `33 passed` — 32 unit + the CPU copy-number check, which lives in the DB-bound module but needs no fixture and RAN). The DB-bound scenario itself stays collected, NOT RUN (the module now collects 12: 11 DB-bound + 1 CPU).

Codex's qualified note, recorded, no action: the SQL NULL handling of the predicate and the Python matcher are not an exact twin for arbitrary malformed JSON nulls (`parameters ->> key IS NULL` admits a JSON null where Python sees a present key); request schemas require strings and every addressed read re-runs the Python matcher, so this is a persisted-data parity limitation, not a new exposure.

Process disclosure, stated plainly: the first edit script for (b) computed its slice offsets before applying two earlier text replacements and wrote a corrupted `test_as_locked.py` (a `SyntaxError` at line 703; `make fmt` rc=2). Nothing was committed in that state: the file was restored from HEAD with `git show HEAD:… >` and the correction re-applied with offsets taken after every other edit, `ast.parse` quoted before the gates. Rule reaffirmed for the lane: take every offset at write time, after all preceding replacements.

### 25.12 addendum — CTL-015 v2 after Codex production-20260921-0214 (the single-owner ruling; commit `fbf4b566`)

Ownership, as it happened: the team lead's 18:39 redirect of CTL-015 to F-CLO crossed B4's own implementation on sprint/l6 (`7092122a`, 18:42:48, `rows.ledger_parts_for` + the test hunk); a 19:12 ruling told F-CLO to drop its hunk, a 19:14 ruling reversed it after Codex 0214 reviewed `eae4078d`, and Codex 0216 confirmed the single owner is F-CLO ("packet 0214's 'existing lane' referred to avoiding duplicate implementation work … Do not create another ownership reversal"). The crossing is the supervisor's, recorded as such; B4 removes its hunk by an ordinary commit; nothing here amends or resets.

Codex 0214 on `eae4078d`: the reconciliation `period_lock_id` expectation is **source-closed**; CTL-015 v1 was incomplete for two concrete reasons, both fixed in `fbf4b566`, both EREV-LED-003 assertions and the committed lock preserved: (a) v1 built the chain with `insert_contract_rows(entity_id=parts.entity_id)`, which re-inserts a `legal_entity` row under the existing id; `_insert_visible` (rows.py 754–764) catches the duplicate-key error and returns a fresh UNINSERTED id, so the chain was not bound to the locked entity and a later EREV-LED-003 could have meant "without a state" for a phantom entity rather than the closed actual scope. v2 builds the parts against the existing entity with plain inserts (customer, singleton group, contract contracting with `parts.entity_id`, engine release, computation — the entity-reuse shape of B4's `7092122a`, credited; no shared helper touched, no error suppressed) and binds the persisted chain to the lock by a positive read before the probe: the contract's `contracting_entity_id` equals the locked entity and `period_state(entity, ASC606, period)` is `closed`. (b) v1's posting insert survived `_fails`' line-only savepoint; `tenant_session` commits, and 0040's deferred posting guard requires a seal, so the correctly refused line would have been followed by a different commit failure. v2 opens one encompassing savepoint around every probe fixture row and rolls it back after the expected refusals; the seal guard is untouched and the lock is re-read `closed` after the rollback. Source-predicted repairs; the test stays **NOT RUN** until the admitted DB execution.
