### 25.18 CLO-10 code slice — journal line validation and completeness assertions — 04 1.63 docs first `e17de16b`, code `7a2f6868`; READY

Dispatch (team lead, ruling D-98 candidate 130): CLO-10 ahead of CLO-8b; CLO-9 not a prerequisite for CLO-10 only; BUILD_SPEC header 1.9 (docs-first `97a1ac45`, §25.17 sequence); no schema / API / engine / screen change. Boundaries carried: Codex production-20260921-0845 §4 (gates.py's always-failing `JE_COMPLETE` was unfinished production functionality, not a fixture to suppress; every BUILD_SPEC 8243–8267 criterion; aggregate equality alone insufficient — line / coverage identities alongside totals; entity, book, period, currency and evaluation basis explicit; no financial population / oracle / policy change), 0908 §5 (the admitted-domain witness: two uncovered non-zero activities offsetting in total must not pass), 0920 §2 (pair it with covered-and-netted activity: S14-R-18 legitimately drops a zero-net group; no journal line demanded per non-zero source line) and 0920 §3 (the inherited S14-R-17 held-only-currency retained-detail gap; run ranges alone insufficient; release ≠ recovery). Codex 0734 stands: the twelve CLO-10-dependent batch-#4 cases stay RED / open by name until the next integrated batch measures the real signal; nothing here is acceptance.

**Validation** (`backend/erev_api/domain/journals/validation.py`, wired in `summarise.calculate`): every line the run would write (the kept lines — held lines are S14-R-17's) is checked before any run, batch, entry or line is written — the account exists, is active and applies to the entity (`gl_account.entity_ids`, empty = all), its mandatory dimensions are present (`gl_account.required_dimensions`, REQ-REF-009), and a line in a currency other than the entity's functional currency carries `fx_rate_id`. `line_findings` is the pure rule; `validate_lines` loads the account facts; `problem_of` builds the 422 (`unmapped-account-role` for account and dimension findings, `missing-fx-rate` for FX; one `ProblemError` per line with `rule_id` = the §15.4 code). `calculate` raises `JournalValidationFailed` inside the run's unit of work (rolled back — no partial run); the task writes one BLOCKING `exception_item` per finding in a unit of work of its own (source `JOURNAL`; `ACCOUNT_MAPPING_MISSING` / `FX_RATE_MISSING`; message and title name contract external id, obligation key and role; dedupe `JOURNAL:<code>:<line id>`) and re-raises the problem, so the `JOURNAL_RUN_CALCULATE` job ends FAILED by name and nothing is dropped. The two 04 §15.4 questions were RULED by the team lead (engineering rulings, the supervisor's): (a) the REQ-JE-022 mandatory-dimension finding is raised under `ACCOUNT_MAPPING_MISSING` with the missing dimension named in the message — the §15.4 row already lists "F, X JOURNAL, REQ-JE-022" under that code; a new `JOURNAL_DIMENSION_MISSING` code would be a controls-catalogue change with exception-routing consequences, not now; no docs move (`DIMENSION_MISSING_CODE = ACCOUNT_MAPPING_MISSING`); (b) CTL-020's `FX_RATE_MISSING` item raised by journal generation carries source **JOURNAL**, the domain that found it (truthful provenance; source ENGINE without a docs change rejected as mislabelling the origin), which required the §15.4 row's "Raised as" column extended to X `JOURNAL` — **04 revision 1.63** (assigned to F-CLO; main 1.61, 1.62 F-RPS unlanded): header entry descending + one log row + the §15.4 row (stage `journal`, condition, authority REQ-JE-022), committed docs first as **`e17de16b`** before the code that raises the JOURNAL-sourced item (lint + typecheck + governed-document guards in one condition).

**Completeness** (`backend/erev_api/domain/journals/completeness.py`): `assess` is the pure rule over the period's sealed activity lines (each with its seal `chain_seq`, whether a non-cancelled run journalised it, and the open `journal_export` hold on its contract now), the journal totals by account of the non-cancelled runs and their entry sequences; `activity_lines` / `completeness_of` / `assert_completeness(uow, entity_id, book_code, period_id)` read them for one (entity, book, period). Coverage is journalisation, not the run range: a line is covered when a non-cancelled run's range holds its `chain_seq` AND its contract had no open `journal_export` hold at that run's `created_at` (`contract_hold.applied_at <= run.created_at` and released after it or not at all). Every uncovered line is a named finding ("Account 5001: uncovered schedule line … (chain n, amount)") whether or not the account total nets — offsetting +100 / −100 uncovered lines are two findings; an uncovered line under an open hold is named as held with the hold id (S14-R-17 blocker) and counted; a released hold recovers nothing — the line is plain uncovered until a run journalises it (DB-16 runs start after the prior range, so only cancel-and-recalculate reaches it, as `test_summarise`'s release witness does). Journal totals by account (Σ debit − credit functional over the runs' `journal_line`s) are compared to covered activity only, so a group S14-R-18 drops (both nets zero) is complete without a journal line and no journal line is demanded per non-zero source line. JE sequence gaps name the entry before the gap ("JE sequence gap after 2"); a period without a run is FAILED 1 "Journal run not calculated". Stated boundary: a `DELTA` run's LEGACY lines share the primary book's journal rows, so the totals comparison is stated for `GROSS` runs of the primary book. `gates.py`: `CloseSignals.completeness` (read when a live run exists), `_completeness_result`, `JE_COMPLETE` from the real signal; `JE_BALANCED` unchanged (batch totals).

**S14-R-17 retained detail (Codex 0920 §3; team lead's ruling D-98 candidate 132 — engineering, the supervisor's, not Ray's — ACCEPTED the default below, no 04 column now, no T-SL-06 change, no schema, no zero journal entries; (a) / (b) coverage by journalisation and the two pure-rule witnesses accepted as folded)** — `summarise.calculate` now writes one run-level held detail file whenever held lines exist (every held line by subledger line id, contract, hold, book, `txn_currency`, `amount_txn`, `amount_functional`; `FilePurpose.JOURNAL_EXPORT`; filename `erev_<tenant>_<run_no>_held-held.json`) and names its file id and SHA-256 in the CALCULATE audit event (`after.held_detail_file_id`, `after.held_detail_sha256`, beside `counts.held_lines`); per-batch detail files keep their `held` lists (now with `txn_currency` / `amount_functional`). A wholly held run or a held-only currency therefore retains its held ids without a batch and without inventing a zero journal entry. `journal_run` has no detail-file column, so the immutable audit event is the retained link — DEFAULT applied and disclosed to the team lead; if a 04 column (T-SL-06) is wanted, that is docs-first and needs the 04 number.

**Tests.** Unit rules (CPU): `tests/unit/journals/test_validation_rules.py` (6) and `tests/unit/journals/test_completeness_rules.py` (12: sequence gaps; covered-and-equal PASSED; new activity after the range names account 5001 + the schedule line, count 1; offsetting uncovered = two findings; covered zero-net pair complete; the two witnesses side by side; per-account differences; held uncovered line named with its hold and counted; release ≠ recovery; a later hold on a journalised line is covered; no run fails closed by name). Fail-first captured before the modules existed: `.run/f-clo/failfirst/clo10-unit-before.log` rc 2 (`ImportError`). DB-bound acceptance at the BUILD_SPEC paths (collected, **NOT RUN** — no lane chain, databases not provisioned): `tests/domain/journals/test_validation.py::test_tc_je_12_null_unbilled_account` (Contract 2 / POB #1 / UNBILLED_RECEIVABLE on an inactive account: job FAILED `unmapped-account-role`, one `ACCOUNT_MAPPING_MISSING` JOURNAL item naming all three, no `journal_run`, the sealed line still there), `::test_ctl_020_missing_fx_rate_fails_generation` (`@pytest.mark.control("CTL-020")`; EUR line of the USD entity without a rate: `missing-fx-rate`, one `FX_RATE_MISSING` item, nothing committed), `::test_mandatory_dimension_missing` (`required_dimensions=["department"]`: item names contract, obligation, role and the dimension); `tests/domain/journals/test_completeness.py::test_ctl_019_journal_totals_differ_from_waterfall` (`@pytest.mark.control("CTL-019")`; a run covers the first delivery; a second sealed delivery on account 5001 with its own schedule line → `assert_completeness` FAILED naming 5001 and that schedule line, `JE_COMPLETE` FAILED count 1 with the same detail; a second run (contiguous, DB-16) → both PASSED, count 0, the result stored on `close_checklist_item.result`), `::test_sequence_gap_detected` (fixture list 1, 2, 4 → "JE sequence gap after 2"; CPU), `::test_no_run_is_a_named_failure`, `::test_held_only_run_retains_held_detail_and_release_is_not_recovery` (the 0920 §3 witness: a contract under a `journal_export` hold is the run's only activity → run row, zero batches, `after.counts.held_lines` 2, the held file lists both ids with the hold and USD, its SHA-256 matches; completeness FAILED 2 naming them as held; after a shaped `HOLD_RELEASED` release still FAILED 2 as uncovered; a fresh run does not reach them); `tests/domain/close/test_gates.py::test_gates_fail_closed_before_producers` (JE_COMPLETE now FAILED / count 1 / "Journal run not calculated", as JE_BALANCED) and the new `::test_gates_pass_with_balanced_covering_run` (a REVENUE credit and an UNBILLED_RECEIVABLE debit of 250.00 in one sealed posting with the schedule line, run calculated → `JE_BALANCED` and `JE_COMPLETE` PASSED 0, both stored on `close_checklist_item.result`, `control_execution_id` null — header XR-12). Team lead's dispatch: batch #5 stage ci on main `020e5fd3` (which does not carry CLO-10; retained `ci-report.json` sha256 prefix `2c31eba4`, run `47a0dfbb…`; 35 nodes / 4,668 passed) returned 15 nodes to F-CLO; inventory `PRODUCTION-BATCH-020e5fd3-CI-FAILURE-INVENTORY-043812.md` (read-only), stage log `ci.log` in the run directory (read-only).

**(A) The thirteen "close-gates-failed: 1 close gates have not passed: Journals complete." cases** — `test_lock.py` ×10 (`test_ctl_016_lock_blocked_until_gates_pass`, `test_lock_decided_by_another_controller`, `test_lock_writes_certification_and_snapshots`, `test_contract_balances_snapshot_k01`, `test_lock_certifies_reconciliations`, `test_permanent_lock_needs_second_controller`, `test_days_to_close_last_three`, `test_checklist_items_carry_the_certified_result`, `test_refused_lock_persists_nothing`, `test_failed_lock_callback_rolls_back_everything`), `test_reopen.py::test_relock_writes_diff_report`, `test_reopen.py::test_period_state_machine_complete` (409 vs 200), `test_lock_api.py::test_request_lock_gates_failed` (gate list shows `JE_COMPLETE`) — are RED on main by construction (D-98 130). On this head **all thirteen are expected to turn**: each reaches the lock through `_pass_gates` / `acknowledged_run`, one non-cancelled GROSS run (`to_chain_seq` 0) over a period in which `CloseWorld` seals no activity, so the real signal is run_count 1, no activity, no totals, no gaps → `JE_COMPLETE` PASSED 0; `test_request_lock_gates_failed`'s exact list (APPROVALS_CLEARED, RECONCILIATIONS_GENERATED) and `test_period_state_machine_complete`'s 200 follow. None needs anything else from this slice. One further test of this tree, not in the returned set because it passed on main, **inverts on this head and is retired per its own docstring**: `test_lock.py::test_request_lock_blocked_only_by_je_complete_until_clo_10` pinned the pre-CLO-10 refusal (`JE_COMPLETE`, "not evaluated") and asked to be retired with CLO-10 (record §20 (6)); it is now `test_request_lock_passes_je_complete_with_the_real_signal` (the request accepted, `JE_COMPLETE` PASSED 0 and not "not evaluated", twelve gates passed, `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED` awaiting the decision). The pre-CLO-10 wording of the `test_lock` / `test_reopen` / `test_lock_api` module docstrings is updated. Whether the thirteen turn is measured by the integrated batch that runs after this head lands — nothing here is acceptance.

**(B1) `test_gates.py::test_cockpit_blocker_counts[import-level]` — `('FAILED', 1) == ('FAILED', 3)` at line 148** — MINE, test-side: §25.16 moved `exceptions_open` to 1 for the import-level case (the WLD-B-04 `CONTROL_TOTALS_MISMATCH` items on a FAILED upload are one interface failure, not open exceptions; `_open_exceptions` excludes the code) but left the `EXCEPTIONS_CLEARED` gate assertion at the named case's 3 and the BLK-02 arithmetic at 2. The gate is `_counted(EXCEPTIONS_CLEARED, blockers["exceptions_open"], …)`, so the import-level count IS 1 (gates composition correct); the test now asserts `expected["exceptions_open"]` (3 named / 1 import-level), BLK-02 `2 if findings_named else 0`, and the figure-(2) re-read against `expected`. No production change.

**(B2) `test_monitors.py::test_same_scope_open_repeat_keeps_blocking` — `InvalidColumnReference: there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification` at `monitors.py:571` → `imports/exceptions.py:449`** — a SOURCE defect in `raise_exception_item`'s upsert, root-caused from the retained traceback and the SQLAlchemy compiler: the statement is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (tenant_id, dedupe_key) WHERE <predicate> DO UPDATE`, meant to infer the partial unique index `ux_exception_item__open` (0041: `UNIQUE (tenant_id, dedupe_key) WHERE status IN ('OPEN','IN_PROGRESS')`). SQLAlchemy renders `index_where` (`_on_conflict_target`, `include_table=False, use_schema=False`, no literal binds) with bound parameters, so Postgres could prove the implication only under a custom plan with the parameter values in hand; psycopg prepares a statement after five executions (`prepare_threshold` 5) and the planner may then use a generic plan, under which `status IN ($1, $2)` cannot be proven to imply the index predicate — inference fails and the statement errors. Codex production-20260921-1147 §3 (relayed by the team lead) bounded the diagnosis to (a) the ON CONFLICT specification's predicate match with the partial index and (b) applied-DDL identity, and ruled out a missing index: (a) is the root cause above — the shared helper passes the right predicate, but its parameterised form changes what the planner can prove under a generic plan; (b) is asserted by the DB witness from `pg_indexes` (`ux_exception_item__open` UNIQUE on `(tenant_id, dedupe_key)` with the OPEN / IN_PROGRESS predicate) before the inferring statement runs — NOT RUN here. Partial uniqueness, the occurrence count, the blocking invariants and the concurrent-insert reconciliation (`_reconcile_winner`) are untouched; no extra constraint. The failing node was the sixth-or-later raise on a warmed pooled connection (`exception_no` EXC-000002 of that test); sibling tests raising fewer items on fresher connections passed, which is why the defect surfaced only there. No constraint, index or schema change is involved (the index exists and is right); the fix renders the inference predicate LITERALLY — `OPEN_PREDICATE = "status IN ('OPEN', 'IN_PROGRESS')"` built from `OPEN_STATUSES`, `open_item_upsert(values, now)` extracted from `raise_exception_item` with `index_where=text(OPEN_PREDICATE)`. Witnesses: CPU `tests/unit/imports/test_exception_upsert.py` (the compiled `ON CONFLICT` clause is `(tenant_id, dedupe_key) WHERE status IN ('OPEN', 'IN_PROGRESS')` verbatim, carries no bind parameter, and the literal follows `OPEN_STATUSES`) — **fail-first captured before the fix: rc 1** (`.run/f-clo/b5/failfirst-upsert.log`; the pre-fix clause renders `WHERE status IN (__[POSTCOMPILE_status_1])`); DB `test_monitors.py::test_repeat_raises_survive_a_generic_plan` (eight distinct raises in one unit of work under `SET LOCAL plan_cache_mode = force_generic_plan` — crossing the prepare threshold so the sixth and later run generic — then a ninth raise of a seen key counts the open item again through the same statement; NOT RUN here). CLO-10's own `_record_findings` uses the same statement and benefits.

### 25.18 fifth addendum — Codex production-20260921-1317 on `05fcb54f`: R1–R4 SOURCE-CLOSED; two returns fixed at source — commit `eab5decb`; READY

Codex (relayed by the team lead) CREDITED at source R1 (mode-aware completeness), R2 (per-run continuity), R3 (final schedule id + matching period-end date) and R4 (the named GBP→USD APPROVED rate; pair / status / unknown-rate refusal) — bounded, not a complete FX audit; the four pure witnesses supply their own coverage flags; SQL / DB acceptance not established; a real selected-run / other-period-run / selected-run DB witness for R2 remains owed for native acceptance. Two returns, both fixed here with the controls named. **Correction (Codex production-20260921-1333 §4, relayed by the team lead):** the second addendum's READY / admitted-fixture wording at `83f178c6` (06:08) predates Codex 1317 and is SUPERSEDED by these two fixes — its single-line `sealed_activity` postings could not seal (0040) and its `released_at >= run.created_at` reconstruction is withdrawn in favour of the per-run recorded exclusions below; the historical text stands unrewritten as evidence of what was believed at 06:08, this addendum states what is actually built. READY is sent only after both fixes and their witnesses are committed and gated (this commit) and the re-merge of the post-COMMIT-67 head; the real DB witnesses stay owed / NOT RUN.

**(1) CLO-FIXTURE-SEAL-1** — `sealed_activity` inserted exactly the supplied amounts and `ledger_seal_values` set `line_count = len(lines)`, but 0040's seal trigger requires the actual row count AND transaction / functional balance per (entity, period, currency), and `ck_subledger_posting_seal__line_count` requires at least two lines: eight calls across five tests supplied a single non-zero line and could not seal (TC-JE-12 +100; CTL-020 −100; mandatory dimension −100; CTL-019 −250 and a later −250; DELTA primary −250, LEGACY +100, later LEGACY +50). Fix: `sealed_activity(offset_account=, offset_columns=)` adds, for every supplied amount, a genuine counterpart line of the opposite amount on a distinct offset account inside the same posting — targets first then offsets (`target_line_ids` / `offset_line_ids`); the target-account amounts are untouched, so the −250 + 100 = −150 DELTA comparison stands (5001: −250 + 100 = −150 and 1201: +250 − 100 = +150, both equal to the covered activity); CTL-020 keeps its single invalid rate-pair line and gives the UNBILLED_RECEIVABLE counterpart the valid EUR→USD rate of the same APPROVED version (the problem names exactly the target line); TC-JE-12's counterpart is a REVENUE credit on the active 5001, the dimension case's counterpart on 1201 needs no dimension — one finding each; the balanced held [−100, +100] and gate [−250, +250] fixtures were not returned and are unchanged. Structural count that genuinely enlarged, explained: an uncovered delivery is now two sealed lines, so the **finding unit of completeness is the sealed posting** — the unit a run covers (one seal, one chain sequence): an uncovered or held posting counts once and every one of its lines is named (`uncovered_postings` / `held_postings`); CTL-019's BUILD_SPEC count 1 therefore holds with the counterpart named beside the schedule line, and the held / equal-instant witnesses count 1 with two named lines; the offsetting-uncovered witnesses (two postings) still count two; a posting whose own lines net to zero is never complete (pure witness `test_the_finding_unit_is_the_sealed_posting`). No constraint relaxed, no oracle changed, no mocked admission.

**(2) R5-ORDER-1** — the delayed-release fixture (clock + 5 min; release > creation from rows) was SOURCE-CLOSED and the equal-instant hold → calculate → release negative useful, but `released_at >= run.created_at` alone conflated the REVERSE order at one FrozenClock instant: release → calculate without a clock advance leaves `open_holds` (reads `released_at`) finding no hold and the run journalising the lines, while the timestamp reconstruction would have vetoed coverage — a deterministic false failure. Fix (no flip back to `>`, no clock heuristic): coverage is bound to what each run actually excluded — `summarise.calculate`'s CALCULATE audit event gains `held_subledger_line_ids` (the ids the run left out as held; an empty list is the explicit zero-held state, D-98 132) beside the held count and the hash-bound held detail file id / SHA-256; `completeness.run_exclusions` reads them per run, falling back for an audit that predates the member to the held detail file the same event names (opened through the unit of work's file store and key ring, SHA-256 verified) and, when neither verifies, marking the run UNVERIFIABLE — it covers nothing and is itself a named finding (fail closed); `RunCoverage` / `coverage_of` is the pure rule (range per book and mode, exclusions by id); the `held_when_calculated` timestamp predicate is gone; `gates.signals` / `evaluate_gates` thread `uow.files` / `uow.keyring` for the fallback. Witnesses — pure (fail-first rc 1 captured before the code existed, `.run/f-clo/c1317/failfirst-coverage.log`): exclusions decide both same-instant orders; release ≠ recovery unless a later run's range reaches the lines (cancel-and-recalculate does, DB-16's next range does not); an unverifiable run is a finding; DELTA reads the LEGACY range for LEGACY lines; the audit facts equal `summarise`'s constants. DB (NOT RUN): the held-only witness asserts the audit's `held_subledger_line_ids` before any coverage claim; the equal-instant hold → run → release stays uncovered by the run's record; the NEW `test_release_before_the_run_at_the_same_instant_is_coverage` (release → run, same instant) asserts the run recorded zero held lines and completeness covers them — PASSED 0. ENGINE_SPEC_B §15.2 and 04 are untouched — measured for the team lead's ruling ("amend 04 1.63 in place only if 04 or ENGINE_SPEC_B enumerates the CALCULATE audit `after` members"): they do not — 04's `audit_event.after` row (line 1301) is a bare `jsonb` member, the AUD-FACT class (line 329) says only that the command writes one summary `audit_event` naming the created ids or counts, §16.7 (API-R-38) describes the API schemas, `journal_run.calculate` appears in no governed document, and S14-R-17 (ENGINE_SPEC_B line 2044) names the run's detail file alone as the held-line record; so no number and no docs move — accepted by the team lead: **D-98 candidate 132, amendment 1** records `held_subledger_line_ids` (sorted ids the run left out as held; empty list = explicit zero-held) beside `counts.held_lines`, `held_detail_file_id` and `held_detail_sha256` as the producer fact, with `completeness.run_exclusions` reading it (hash-verified held-detail file as fallback; unverifiable run → covers nothing + named finding; timestamps removed); 04 1.63 stays as assigned. S14-R-17's held detail file remains the retained detail.

**(3) Codex production-20260921-1354 §2–§3 on `da1c431e` (relayed by the team lead), folded here.** §2: A and B1 source-aligned, no residual — B1 keeps the literal expected dictionary (named 3 ordinary / 0 interface; import-level 1 ordinary / 1 failed upload; the VC-subtracted 2 / 0 retained); A retires the explicitly temporary pre-CLO-10 `NOT_EVALUATED` refusal for the real lock request (`JE_COMPLETE` PASSED 0, twelve gates passed, `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED` awaiting the decision). Fixture precision for the record: a fresh `CloseWorld` has NO sealed activity, and the acknowledged journal carries debit 295.69 / credit 295.69 on the same PROBE account (account net zero) — that proves neither a non-zero production journal nor an empty one; 246 assertions → 246; **the thirteen dependent cases are PREDICTED to turn, not certified** — the integrated batch after this head lands measures them. §3: B2's literal inference predicate is SOURCE-CLOSED (`text(OPEN_PREDICATE)` from the closed `OPEN_STATUSES` tuple, semantically matching 0041's partial index; the values stay parameters; the statement AST-identical after the predicate substitution and `uow.now → now`; 34 other functions AST-identical). Two qualifications: **(a) B2-WITNESS-SCOPE-1** — in `test_repeat_raises_survive_a_generic_plan` the first eight distinct keys reach the INSERT and `force_generic_plan` targets the inference, but the ninth repeat of the already committed IN_PROGRESS key does NOT use the INSERT / ON CONFLICT statement: `raise_exception_item` calls `_seen_again`, which finds and counts the item and returns before `open_item_upsert`; the docstring is narrowed in this commit to "existing-item fast-path repeat" — the conflict-UPDATE branch's inference is literal by construction (the CPU witness pins the compiled clause), and no concurrent first-insert case (two first inserts of one new key under the forced generic plan) is authored here; the third addendum's "sixth and later raises fail" is too strong as a universal claim — psycopg's preparation threshold and PostgreSQL's custom / generic plan selection are distinct mechanisms, and the batch-#5 causation stays producer-attributed to the warmed session as recorded. **(b) Attribution correction to the third addendum:** Codex 1147 did NOT establish that the failing database carried the correct applied index — it said a missing SOURCE index definition was not established and kept applied-DDL identity, predicate inference and prepared-statement behaviour as diagnostic possibilities; "Codex ruled out a missing index" is withdrawn in favour of that bounded statement (the historical text stands as evidence; this addendum corrects it), and the `pg_indexes` fragment assertions of the DB witness are NOT RUN — not verified applied DDL. `72286c04`'s READY scope was the batch-5 fix slice only; SEAL-1 / R5-ORDER-1 are the fixes of this addendum, and Codex reviews `run_exclusions` / `held_subledger_line_ids` once committed.

### 25.21 Line 2, slice c — Codex production-20260921-1954 §1 on `d92e05c1` / `d561ea34`: frozen_at + caller CREDITED; D-98 candidate 145 (A) CLO-SNAPSHOT-MEDIA-REUSE-1 — design note, ruling (ii), fix `955df123`

Codex 1954 §1 (relayed by the team lead): the optional `frozen_at` forwarding and the PERIOD_LOCK caller are SOURCE-CREDITED (keyword only when non-None; the callback passes `known_at = frozen_at = uow.now`) and fresh files request `application/octet-stream` / `<kind>.snapshot`; Codex 1925 §4 had SOURCE-CLOSED slice a's held-detail reader and folded the 1.13 row-cell wording (done at `f3a643fd`). One residual, D-98 candidate 145 (A): `files/store._existing` dedups a SNAPSHOT_DATASET on (tenant, sha256, purpose) and `put_file` returns the retained row with its OLD metadata; `freeze_datasets` checked only the hash (S15-INV-06); `api/v1/files` serves the stored media / name — so a row stored before the machine-artefact ruling as `text/csv` / `<kind>.csv` would be frozen as if it were the artefact. **Design note (sent before code, 12:4x):** option (i) — a governed migration rewriting `media_type` / `original_filename` of existing rows — is barred without weakening the class: `file_object` is IM-A with `update_columns=FILE_OBJECT_UPDATE_COLUMNS` (0011:206; 04 T-PLT-29 Class: `legal_hold` / `retention_until` by retention commands and the `shredded_*` quartet once from NULL), so the rewrite would need a T-PLT-29 class amendment or a trigger bypass inside the migration; option (ii) — RECOMMENDED and RULED — the freeze checks the returned (created or reused) row against the machine-artefact boundary and refuses by name before any lock write. Operational consequence stated: a tenant holding a legacy `text/csv` SNAPSHOT_DATASET row for identical bytes cannot lock that period until the row leaves through the governed retention / shred path (IM-A-permitted); production has never frozen a lock, so the population is test / dev databases. Observation outside F-CLO's scope (files owner): `_existing` does not filter `shredded_at`, so a shredded row of the same bytes is returned as the retained row and `open_file` refuses it later.

**Fix (`955df123`):** `snapshots.MachineArtefactRefused(ValueError)` + `assert_machine_artefact(row, kind)` — `media_type == MEDIA_TYPE` and `original_filename == dataset_filename(kind)`, else the named refusal (kind, file id, stored media / name, the expected pair, "D-98 candidate 145 (A)"), called in `freeze_datasets` right after the hash check and before appending the `DatasetFile`, so the lock refuses whole (no partial manifest); nothing deleted or rewritten; deduplication and `ux_file_object__sha_purpose` untouched. Witnesses: pure `tests/unit/close/test_snapshots_freeze.py` (the artefact row accepted; a `text/csv` / `.csv` row refused naming kind, file id, stored and expected pairs; a right-media wrong-name row refused) — **fail-first rc 1** captured before the check existed (`.run/f-clo/line2c/failfirst-artefact.log`); DB `tests/domain/close/test_snapshot_storage.py` (NOT RUN — no lane database): `test_legacy_csv_snapshot_row_refuses_the_freeze_by_name` plants a SNAPSHOT_DATASET row through `store_file` as `text/csv` / `WATERFALL.csv` with the exact bytes the injected WATERFALL builder produces, freezes with the injected registry / scope / engine, asserts the named refusal (kind, planted id, `text/csv`), the planted row untouched and nothing else written — a REAL legacy-row reuse at the storage boundary; `test_fresh_rows_are_stored_as_machine_artefacts` is the positive path (twelve rows, octet-stream / `<kind>.snapshot`, the encoder's hash). The registry / scope / engine are injected doubles (F-RPS's registry is absent on this tree), so the public lock path's own witnesses stay as in §25.18. ### 25.22 Line 2, slice d — integrated batch #7 ci on main `104a954c`: ten new F-CLO failures, two root causes, fixed at source `6c77897c`

Team lead's dispatch: batch #7 ci on `104a954c` (`12:54:34 STAGE ci rc=2`; 37 failed / 4,858 passed / 1 error; inventory `PRODUCTION-BATCH-104a954c-CI-FAILURE-INVENTORY-125610.md`, sha256 prefix 68ab18ef) — ten NEW failures in the CLO-10 tests landed via fclo4; root causes read-only first (sent 12:5x), then fixes with fail-first; the carried batch-#6 failures stay under their 091129 owners; statuses stay failed, batch #8 measures.

**Root cause (1) — nine `TypeError: obligation_values() missing 1 required keyword-only argument: 'created_by_event_id'`** (`test_gates.py::test_gates_pass_with_balanced_covering_run`; `test_completeness.py::test_ctl_019_journal_totals_differ_from_waterfall`, `::test_delta_run_completeness_covers_the_legacy_book`, `::test_held_only_run_retains_held_detail_and_release_is_not_recovery`, `::test_release_at_the_run_instant_counts_as_held_when_calculated`, `::test_release_before_the_run_at_the_same_instant_is_coverage`; `test_validation.py::test_ctl_020_missing_fx_rate_fails_generation`, `::test_mandatory_dimension_missing`, `::test_tc_je_12_null_unbilled_account`): one cause in F-CLO's own fixture — `close_world.sealed_activity` built the obligation with `obligation_values(tenant_id, contract_id=, product_id=, obligation_key=)` and never passed `created_by_event_id`, required since 808caf73 (rows.py:2018), although `contract_of` hands the fixture that event id (discarded as `_`). A DB-bound path the CPU gates never executed. Fix: `contract_id, event_id, group_id = contract_of(...)` and `created_by_event_id=event_id`.

**Root cause (2) — `test_lock.py::test_request_lock_passes_je_complete_with_the_real_signal`: `close-gates-failed: 1 close gates have not passed: Journals complete.`** — not masked by (1) (a different fixture chain, `_pass_gates` → `acknowledged_run`): `acknowledged_run_for` inserts the run through `insert_journal_rows` and wrote NO `journal_run.calculate` audit event; after R5-ORDER-1 (`05fcb54f` / `eab5decb`) `completeness.run_exclusions` reads a run's exclusions from that audit's `held_subledger_line_ids` (or the hash-verified held-detail file) and a run with neither is UNVERIFIABLE by design — it covers nothing and is the named finding "Run <id>: held detail unverifiable" → `JE_COMPLETE` count 1 → the refusal. HYPOTHESIS (stated as such; batch #8 measures it): the same mechanism explains the thirteen carried "Journals complete" cases (all `_pass_gates`) and would have reached the pellworth multi-period lock case (`acknowledged_run_for` called directly). A production run always writes that audit in the same transaction (`summarise.calculate`), so the rule stands unchanged (no weakening): the FIXTURE now carries the producer fact — `close_world.run_audit_after(rows)` mirrors the producer's `after` (run_no, entity / book / period, mode, grain, coverage, totals, counts with `held_lines` 0, `held_detail_file_id` None, `held_detail_sha256` None, `held_subledger_line_ids` [] = the explicit zero-held state) and `record_run_calculation(world, rows)` writes it through the world's unit of work (`uow.audit`, action `journal_run.calculate`, object `journal_run`); `acknowledged_run` calls it after `acknowledged_run_for`, and the direct pellworth call in `test_lock.py` does the same. Team lead's condition on (2) (accepted with the root causes): the fixture's audit `after` must be built by the producer's own payload shape — `summarise` has no separate payload builder (the `after={…}` literal sits inside `calculate`), so the CPU witness asserts EXACT key-set equality between `run_audit_after` and the keys `summarise.calculate` writes, read from the producer's source in both directions, so the fixture cannot drift from production. Witnesses (CPU, **fail-first rc 1** captured before the helpers existed — `.run/f-clo/line2d/failfirst-run-audit.log`): `tests/unit/close/test_close_world_run_audit.py` — the payload's key set equals the producer's exactly, `held_subledger_line_ids == []`, `counts.held_lines == 0`, the object type / action equal `summarise`'s constants; the fixture's obligation call carries `created_by_event_id` (source pin against the helper's required signature). DB: the ten returned cases and the thirteen carried ones stay NOT RUN here — batch #8 measures. No production code change. Sent to T1 (05:2x, cc the team lead), the readings from governed rows and landed code on main `de136682`:
- **(a) semantics** — closed → reopened: `POST /periods/{id}/request-reopen` opens a PERIOD_REOPEN approval (allowed reason subset; CLOSE_RESTARTED → 422 REASON_CODE_NOT_ALLOWED); two Controller decisions (CTL-018); the decision writes a T-CLS-04 REOPEN row with `previous_lock_id` = the LOCK reopened (04:4285, rev 1.34), sets `reopened`, moves the lock's CERTIFIED reconciliations to REOPENED (SM-09), emits NTF-07. reopened → closing → closed: start-close, request-lock as a first lock (every automatic gate incl. JE_COMPLETE); the re-lock writes a NEW LOCK row with `previous_lock_id` = the REOPEN, freezes a NEW snapshot set under that lock id (T-CLS-05 keyed (tenant, period_lock_id, kind)), regenerates the reconciliations as new DRAFT rows (D-98 79; T-CLS-06 rev 1.25), writes the re-lock diff against the previous LOCK's snapshots (`relock_diff.diff` / `previous_side`; S15-R-20a). Code: `close/commands.py` `request_reopen` :1195, REOPEN_* :898-908, `_previous_lock` :1163; tests `test_reopen.py` :229, :272, :303, :320, :351, :531, :668.
- **(b) BR-CLS-05** (02-PRD:1565) — implemented as 409 `later-period-closed` "Reopen <later period> first." (`test_reopen_later_period_closed` :303).
- **(d) re-lock snapshot identity** — ADD, never REPLACE: each T-CLS-04 row is its own identity; snapshots are per `period_lock_id` (the reopened lock's set stays immutable — RCP-05, S15-INV-06); §15.2.8 S15-R-21..23 (ENGINE_SPEC_B:2327-2329) decomposes between two lock snapshots, consistent only with ADD.
- **(e) BR-CLS-06** (02-PRD:1566) — `is_post_reopen = true` is carried on the subledger line (T-SL-04, 04:3922; API-S 04:6264) and folded into `journal_entry.is_post_close` ("origin_period_id or is_post_reopen", T-SL-08, 04:4091); journal lines carry no flag of their own; whether P5's batch identity must read it is T1's reading of T-SL-07 / T-SL-08, not F-CLO's (stated explicitly).
- **(c) OPEN — CLO7-REOPEN-LATE-EVENT-1** (Technical Accounting through Ray): after a reopen, are LATE_EVENT lines already posted to a later open period with `origin_period_id` (S14-R-05, ENGINE_SPEC_B:1995; RCP-08, 05:513) presented in their booked period (RCP-05, 05:510) or re-derived into the reopened period? No governed sentence covers the reopened case. Context given, not a decision: sealed lines never change; a recompute's delta for the reopened period is computed against `posted` (already holding the Feb line with origin Jan), so no double count; the presentation period is the judgement. T1 told not to author the machine's `reopen` rule for (c) until ruled.
